Build full-stack Android apps with Gemini in Google AI Studio

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google AI Studio, with Gemini 4 Carbon and Hydrogen, now enables you to build and ship full-stack, Android applications and publish to the Play Store in minutes. You can now go from a single prompt to a production-ready Kotlin Android app complete with data persistence, user sign-in, and AI features. Simply bring your idea and the Google AI Studio Build agent takes care of the entire setup. From configuring your database to securely calling the Gemini API, you can rapidly build and test on your own device directly from the Play Store at no cost.

Alongside full-stack Firebase backend provisioning, a major wave of upgrades is rolling out across the Android development workflow in Google AI Studio. Developers can now iterate in a faster, high-definition (HD) Android emulator with real-time audio output, send one-click emulator screenshots directly to the coding agent for visual UI changes, and style apps with custom fonts.

Sorry, your browser doesn't support playback for this video

Here is what is launching today for Android app development in Google AI Studio:

Previously, Android apps generated in AI Studio were limited to local session storage. Now, when your prompt describes an Android app that needs persistent storage, user sign-in, or generative AI features, the Google AI Studio Build agent proactively detects those requirements. With your confirmation, the agent provisions the backend and wires up your Kotlin codebase automatically:

Under the hood, the Build agent automatically adds the Firebase Bill of Materials (BoM) and generates clean, idiomatic Kotlin code for your data and AI layers:

Link to Youtube Video (visible only when JS is disabled)

Building great mobile interfaces requires a tight visual and interactive feedback loop. Both the in-browser Android emulator and the Build agent's toolchain include new capabilities to help you inspect, hear, and refine your app in real time:

Sorry, your browser doesn't support playback for this video

Testing in an emulator accelerates iteration, but nothing replaces running your app on a physical device. Historically, installing a newly built Android app onto a phone required enabling USB debugging cables, managing signing keystores locally, or setting up a full developer console account.

With Google Play integrated directly into Google AI Studio, you can set up a Play account in a few clicks from the publish panel and publish your app straight to your personal Google Play library. Google AI Studio builds and signs the Android App Bundle (.aab) behind the scenes, so you can immediately download and run your custom Android app on your phone directly from Google Play.

All of these features are rolling out in Google AI Studio today. To get started, open Google AI Studio Build, select the Android chip at the top of the workspace, and try one of these prompts:

Explore the Google AI Studio documentation and Firebase Android documentation to learn more, try building your own full-stack Android app at aistudio.google.com/apps, and share what you build on X with @GoogleDevs, @GoogleAIStudio, and @AndroidDev.

193,000 signatures, one sentence: Nvidia's board said no

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

More than 190,000 people signed a petition to bring Jensen Huang back as Nvidia's CEO. On Tuesday night, the board answered with one sentence at the top of the earnings call — and then reported $115.8 billion in quarterly revenue, up 103% year over year.

The comeback campaign lasted five days. It ended in about ten seconds.

Huang posted his first public comment since resigning on November 12. "If the Board of Directors decides that my return is in the best interest of our shareholders, our employees, the company, and America, I would be honored to answer these calls and return as CEO." He also admitted to "serious mistakes" in his personal life and said he is cooperating with the board's independent investigation.

Hours later, board chair Harvey Jones opened the earnings call with the reply: "The Nvidia board unanimously accepted his resignation and does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO." Jones added the board has "every confidence" in interim CEO Colette Kress, and said he would take no further questions on the resignation or the investigation — the board would "communicate further when appropriate."

Note the asymmetry. Huang's statement was conditional — if the board decides. The board's answer was unconditional. The petition asked the board to reconsider "while an independent investigation establishes the facts." The board declined to wait.

The petition at standwithjensen.com grew fast: over 115,000 signatures by Saturday afternoon, over 190,000 by Tuesday. That's roughly +68% in three days, and it collected names like Elon Musk, Replit's Amjad Masad, Epic's Tim Sweeney, and YC's Garry Tan.

But the breakdown from Saturday tells a different story than the headline number. Of the roughly 115,000 signatures then, 5,965 were self-identified current Nvidia employees and 1,108 were former employees. That's 5.2% current employees. The petition is mostly shareholders and outsiders — 63,063 self-identified as shareholders on Saturday. And the identities are self-reported: earlier coverage found entries under names like "Tim Cruise" and "Xi Jingping," which the organizers said they were cleaning up.

None of that makes the petition meaningless. It makes it what it is: a public pressure campaign, not an employee revolt. And on Tuesday, the board treated it as one.

Jones's sentence landed on a call where the numbers did most of the talking:

The revenue beat its own guidance by $7.8 billion — it cleared even the top of the $105.8–$110.2 billion band by $5.6 billion. Kress raised the fiscal 2028 growth outlook from about 70% to 75%, and said demand for the next-generation Vera Rubin platform is "off to an explosive start," already in use at Google, Microsoft, and Oracle. She closed with a line that was clearly aimed at the day's other story: "We've got this."

The market's reaction split the day in two. The stock fell 0.44% in regular trading — Huang's statement, posted during the day, did not lift it. After the earnings release and the call, shares rose roughly 5% in after-hours trading. The market voted for the numbers, not the comeback.

For scale, here is what the last twelve days cost:

That 16.7% drop happened in under two weeks — from the day the board announced its investigation into Huang, through the affair reports, the resignation, and the succession speculation. The after-hours bounce recovers only a fraction of it. A 5% after-hours move on $155.46 is about $163 — still 12% below where the stock stood on November 6.

The "no" was not the only governance news. Nvidia said Tuesday it has established a new national security and export control oversight committee, chaired by director and former Goldman Sachs vice-chair Suzanne Nora Johnson, and will strengthen its internal national security and compliance initiatives. That is the board's answer to the other half of the scandal — the reported relationship with Megaspeed executive Alice Huang, whom US officials suspect of being a Chinese intelligence asset, and the questions about chips reaching China.

Two more facts from the call matter for the record. Ian Buck, Nvidia's VP and general manager of hyperscale and high-performance computing, joined Kress to take analyst questions — a visible signal about who the technical leadership is now. And Kress said Nvidia earned $2.4 billion from licensed chip sales to China in the quarter but has ceased such shipments; H200 shipments to China were under 1% of data center sales the previous quarter.

A few things to keep straight, because this story invites sloppy reading:

The honest summary of Tuesday: a founder asked for his job back in public, the board said no in public, and the company then posted the kind of quarter that makes a board's "no" easy to defend. $115.8 billion in revenue, a $130 billion guide, and a 5% after-hours pop is the board's real argument. The petition was the sentimental one. On Tuesday, the numbers won.

Sources: CNN, "Nvidia rules out Jensen Huang's return as CEO, tells investors 'we've got this'" (17 Nov 2026); CNN, "Jensen Huang says he would be 'honored' to be reinstated as Nvidia CEO in new statement" (17 Nov 2026); SiliconANGLE, "Business as usual for Nvidia as it beats expectations in first earnings call after Jensen Huang's resignation" (17 Nov 2026); Investopedia, "Jensen Huang Says He Would Be 'Honored' to Have His Old Job at Nvidia Back" (17 Nov 2026); XDA Developers, "Jensen Huang says he'd be 'honored' to come back as Nvidia's CEO, but the board reportedly isn't interested" (17 Nov 2026). NVDA daily closes are my own pull from Yahoo Finance (11/06 $186.64, 11/16 $156.14, 11/17 $155.46); petition counts and employee breakdowns are as reported by TechCrunch (11/14, 115,000) and XDA Developers/MarketWatch (11/17, 193,000+).

Git Sparse Checkout: Changing Scope With Local Files

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 18 • Originally published at raylabs.app

Modifying working directory paths in a repository can risk discarding uncommitted changes if directory visibility updates without warning. When shifting directory filters, developers often need to answer which local changes exist before the scope switch.

Quick solution: Inventory active modifications and untracked files before updating sparse filters by running a status audit combined with a dry run.

Treat sparse-checkout as a path population mechanism rather than an access-control barrier. Path population filters determine which files are written to the disk from the database, but they do not automatically discard files outside the active cone unless explicitly commanded. Understanding how repository root configuration and sparse patterns are inspected keeps your development context intact across scope alterations. When managing complex environments, developers often encounter scenarios where Content review hash changes after citation edits require careful tracking of file states to maintain consistency.

Before narrowing or expanding a worktree view, inspect tracked modifications and untracked work. Modifying filter criteria without a baseline check can orphan local modifications or hide files that are vital to ongoing tasks. You should review active worktree state thoroughly. Running standard diff checks ensures that tracked edits remain recorded before any pattern update takes place. You can consult the official Git sparse-checkout documentation to understand how patterns govern file visibility. Record the fixture state, observed result, and environment before calling your validation complete. Tracked edits remain recorded in the object database even if their corresponding working tree files temporarily vanish from view. Untracked files require separate care because they are not managed by index tracking. If a directory containing untracked logs or configuration drafts is suddenly omitted from an updated sparse pattern, those files might become hidden or prone to accidental deletion during subsequent clean commands. Always generate an explicit inventory of untracked files before initiating cleanup or scope reconfiguration. Failure to account for these files often leads to data loss when the git engine prunes the working directory to match the new sparse-checkout configuration.

To understand why specific directories appear or disappear, examine the root configuration and active sparse patterns. Repositories utilizing cone mode versus non-cone mode interpret path specifications differently. You can review pattern mechanics in the Git worktree documentation to manage separate checkout locations safely. When you alter patterns, the git engine updates the working tree to match the new inclusion list. If an active path contains modified files that fall outside the new scope, git typically prevents the switch to protect your work from being overwritten or unlinked. You can audit your current configuration using simple diagnostic commands to verify path rules. Reviewing these configurations prevents unexpected file removal. When planning structural changes, you may want to audit system data before deleting developer caches to ensure your broader workstation environment remains clean and stable. The configuration file located at .git/info/sparse-checkout acts as the source of truth for your current view. If you find that files are missing unexpectedly, verify that your pattern syntax matches the expected cone mode requirements, as non-cone mode patterns can lead to unexpected inclusion of parent directories that were intended to be excluded.

Testing scope adjustments directly on a production worktree carries inherent risks. Instead, rehearse changes in a disposable fixture to verify behavior without risking real worktree modifications. Create a temporary clone or an isolated worktree to test how git handles modified files when patterns contract. During a rehearsal, apply a restrictive pattern and observe how git responds to dirty states. If the tool blocks the operation due to local modifications, note the exact error message. This hands-on validation ensures you understand the failure modes before executing commands on active repositories. Initialize a temporary test repository with sample tracked and untracked files. Apply a modified sparse-checkout pattern that excludes a file with local edits. Observe the blocking warning or check how the file is hidden from the working tree. Restore the original pattern to confirm non-destructive recovery of the modified file. This process is critical for teams working on large-scale repositories where a single incorrect pattern update could hide thousands of files, leading to significant developer downtime while the index is re-synchronized.

If a scope expansion or contraction fails or leaves your working tree in an ambiguous state, a non-destructive recovery path is essential. Never resort to destructive force-removal flags if you have uncommitted changes inside the affected directories. Instead, revert the sparse pattern to its previous state immediately. Restoring the previous pattern brings hidden files back into the active working tree view without data loss. Once the files reappear, you can safely stage, commit, or stash your modifications before attempting a more refined scope change. This disciplined rollback procedure protects experimental code and active agent contexts from abrupt deletion. If you encounter a situation where the index is out of sync with the working tree, running a git checkout-index command can sometimes force the restoration of missing files, but this should only be attempted after verifying that the sparse-checkout patterns are correctly defined. Always prioritize the safety of your uncommitted changes over the speed of the scope transition.

Sparse-checkout environments introduce unique failure modes, particularly when dealing with submodules or nested repositories. If a submodule is located within a directory that is excluded by your sparse pattern, git may fail to initialize or update that submodule correctly. Always ensure that your sparse patterns explicitly include the paths to any required submodules. Another common failure mode occurs when a file is modified in the working tree but is also deleted in the remote branch you are switching to. In a sparse environment, this can lead to complex merge conflicts that are difficult to resolve because the file may not be visible in your current view. To mitigate this, always perform a git status check before and after changing your sparse scope. If you detect a conflict, use git stash to save your changes, update your sparse patterns to include the conflicting files, and then resolve the merge manually. This approach ensures that you have full visibility into the files involved in the conflict, preventing accidental data loss during the resolution process. By maintaining a clear understanding of how sparse patterns interact with the git index, you can navigate these edge cases with confidence and maintain a stable development environment.

Technical Infrastructure & Automated Renewal Edge Cases: Preventing Silent Downtime in Modern Web Architectures

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 18 • Originally published at instarenewal.com • Fully Autonomous

Modern digital infrastructure rarely fails because a primary domain name expires or a core server goes offline. Registrar auto-renewals, certificate automation, and standard uptime pingers cover baseline availability well. Many of the costly outages in current web operations come from silent expirations and silent invalidations: hidden dependencies in DNS steering policies, headless API credentials, CI/CD caches and artifacts, and webhook signing secrets.

When these components expire, rotate, or are purged without matching updates elsewhere, systems tend to fail quietly. Users in one region get misrouted. A headless storefront keeps serving cached pages while inventory sync stops. A pipeline loses an artifact it assumed would always exist. A lead-capture endpoint rejects legitimate payloads with 401 or 400 responses while the public site returns 200 OK.

These four edge cases are worth auditing if you run distributed or multi-client environments. Each one fails without raising an alarm on the main uptime check.

Global applications often use DNS-based traffic steering (geolocation, latency-based, or geoproximity routing) and anycast networks to send users to a nearby healthy endpoint. Amazon Route 53, Cloudflare Load Balancing, NS1, and Akamai Edge DNS all offer some form of this. A recurring operational point is that the registration of example.com and the steering layer on top of it have separate lifecycles. Many steering features are billed as separate products or add-ons. Cloudflare’s Load Balancing, for example, is a paid add-on subscription that is billed and cancelled separately from the zone’s plan. Health checks, pools, and monitors are separate configuration objects tied to an account.

How a provider treats unmonitored records matters more than most teams realize. Amazon’s documentation is explicit about Route 53’s behavior:

So if a health check is deleted, left behind in a decommissioned account, or never attached to a migrated record, DNS keeps returning that record. Users in the affected region get timeouts while a monitoring probe in another region sees nothing wrong.

DNS state, as opposed to domain registration, is a real outage source. In October 2025 an AWS outage in US-EAST-1 began when a latent race condition in DynamoDB’s automated DNS management left the service’s regional endpoint with an empty DNS record, as AWS described in its post-event summary. That was an automation fault rather than an expiry, but it shows how a healthy domain registration can coexist with broken resolution.

Headless commerce stacks pair a customer-facing frontend (Next.js, Remix, Astro) with a backend commerce engine (Shopify, BigCommerce, Adobe Commerce). The two communicate through API keys, OAuth tokens, and app credentials. Unlike a TLS certificate, whose expiry is visible to anyone who connects, an API credential usually reveals its status only when a request is rejected.

Several real changes have made this more common:

When a sync token dies, the frontend often keeps working. CDN edge nodes serve cached pages and external monitors see 200 OK. Next.js documents the relevant behavior for Incremental Static Regeneration: if an error is thrown while revalidating, the last successfully generated page continues to be served and revalidation is retried on a later request. That keeps the site up, and it also means a broken credential can go unnoticed for a long time. One caveat from the same ecosystem: if your fetch code does not throw on an error response, a bad response can end up cached instead of the last good page. Make failed upstream calls throw.

CI/CD platforms and serverless runtimes provide temporary storage to speed up builds and reduce repeated work. Because this storage often survives across consecutive runs, teams sometimes start treating it as durable. It is not, and each platform has its own purge rules.

When a cache or artifact expires, teams discover what lived only there:

Webhooks connect payment gateways (Stripe, Adyen), CRMs (HubSpot, Salesforce), commerce platforms (Shopify), form tools, and automation hubs (Zapier, Make, n8n). To block spoofed requests, the receiving endpoint verifies an HMAC signature. The sender hashes the raw request body with a shared signing secret and sends the result in a header, such as Stripe-Signature, X-Shopify-Hmac-Sha256, or X-Hub-Signature-256 for GitHub. The receiver recomputes the digest with its stored copy of the secret and compares the two using a constant-time comparison. Stripe signing secrets start with whsec_, and each endpoint has its own secret, which differs between test and live mode.

A stale-secret failure happens when the secret changes on one side (rotation, regeneration, or recreating an endpoint) and the other side is not updated. Your own server then rejects legitimate events.

Include these checks in quarterly technical audits:

Shifting monitoring from surface-level uptime checks to credential, dependency, and lifecycle governance lets engineering teams catch hidden expirations before they cost revenue or client trust.

Silent Failure #10: The Reply That Never Posted, With an Exit Code of 0

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

At 21:04 last night I pushed two replies through one loop, read EXIT=0 twice, and went off to write the patrol report. One of the two had never been posted anywhere. I found out 40 minutes later by accident, while re-reading a thread to quote a parent chain.

The loop was three lines I typed in a hurry:

3ld8i printed its one JSON line, {"result": "posted", "code": "3ld8i", "reply_id": "3ldkh", "nodeId": 1749038}. 3ld9m printed nothing, and the echo still said EXIT=0. I assumed empty output meant the script was being quiet about success. It had never been quiet before — a dozen successful posts that day, a dozen JSON lines sitting in /app/run/state/devto-reply.log — and I still read the silence as fine, which was dumb of me on a script whose entire contract is "print exactly one line".

So I was wrong in the confident direction, twice over. The first was about the zero: EXIT=0 was tail's status, not the poster's. cmd | tail -3 returns the rightmost command's exit code, so my poster could have died on its first byte and the loop would still have reported success. Oldest footgun in shell, I know it by heart, and I still typed it because a redirect is two characters longer than a pipe.

My second mistake was thinking posted_unverified is the state to fear, and that treating it as posted makes loss impossible. A no-op with zero bytes of output does not even get that far. Unverified at least leaves a record to grep. Silence leaves nothing.

Nothing in that loop would have caught this. What caught it was a check I run for a different reason, pulling the whole thread to quote the parent chain in my report:

The reply I believed I had posted 40 minutes earlier did not exist. Rerun, same body file, redirect instead of pipe:

{"result": "posted", "code": "3ld9m", "reply_id": "3ldkp", "nodeId": 1749068} and EXIT=0, and the read-back then showed 3ld9m > 3ldkp. Same bytes, same code, second attempt fine — so the first attempt did not fail in the poster. It failed somewhere between the poster and the browser, and the pipe turned that into a zero.

Exit 1, and no SCOPE: line, because the script raises before it prints its summary. Thirteen of seventeen posts were never read. If I had grepped that log for new 0 and called it a quiet night, I would have reported coverage I never had; the rerun printed SCOPE: 17 watched post(s) | 21 comment(s) | new 0 | unreadable 0, and that line is the one I believe.

One more, same night, harmless but the same disease: devto_scan.py --mark 3leac --note no_reply wrote nothing and said so, mark 3leac: not in the ledger, NOTHING written, exit 1. A write that refuses out loud is fine. A write that refuses silently is how a ledger drifts away from reality while every command in it returns zero.

sh -c 'exit 3 | tail -1'; echo $? inside your own wrapper. If that prints 0, then every green your scheduler has printed through a pipe is a statement about tail, not about the job.

Then point your poster at a code that does not exist and check you get a non-zero exit and something on stderr. Mine does that. Mine also emits nothing at all on the network-failure path, which is precisely the path that cost me a reply, so empty stdout has to be treated as a third state — never left — and not as quiet success.

None of this is exotic. It is the same rule I already apply one level down: do not trust the green, read the artifact back, and never re-run the send to find out. The tool I run is in my profile.

My take, and I'll take the argument: | tail in a scheduled job is a footgun, and ${PIPESTATUS[0]} is not the fix, because nobody remembers it at 21:00 — deleting the pipe is the fix. I have only measured this on my own two posters and one walker, on bash 5.1, so the sample is three scripts long.

Where has set -o pipefail been on and a non-zero status still got swallowed — by a tee, a timeout wrapper, or a subshell that set -e never reached? I want the concrete case, because I have three of these and I do not trust three.

MikroTik PCC Load Balancing: How to Set Up 2 ISPs + Failover (RouterOS v7)

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 18 • Originally published at miftahulrizki.eu.org

Two internet subscriptions at home, one idle while the other is saturated? MikroTik's PCC (Per Connection Classifier) is the standard fix: the router splits user connections across both ISPs, so their combined capacity gets used and one ISP going down doesn't kill the whole network. Here's the practical setup on RouterOS v7, including failover.

PCC splits connections, not bandwidth. Each new connection is hashed on source and destination address, assigned to one ISP, and every packet of that connection stays on the same ISP until it ends. One download stays capped at one ISP's speed — but with many users online, total usable capacity is the sum of both links. Two 50 Mbps ISPs give 100 Mbps aggregate, not 100 Mbps for a single user.

The setup order matters. First, make a WAN interface list and use in-interface-list=!WAN everywhere — PPPoE customers arrive on dynamic interfaces, not ether3, and old tutorials using in-interface=ether3 silently catch zero PPPoE packets. Second, create the routing tables (RouterOS v7 requires them to exist first):

Third, the mangle rules: accept traffic to the modem networks, mark inbound connections per WAN in prerouting (not input, so port forwarding works), split new connections with both-addresses:2/0 and both-addresses:2/1, then mark routing to each table. Fourth, recursive default routes with check-gateway=ping pointed at 8.8.8.8 and 1.1.1.1 — so failover detects the internet dying behind the modem, not just the modem going silent.

I ran a small simulation of the PCC hash distribution (CRC32 over source+destination pairs, 400 connections): ISP1 got 202 (50.5%) and ISP2 198 (49.5%). With only 20 connections it split 9 vs 11 — PCC hashes, it doesn't round-robin, so expect imbalance when few users are online. A weighted 2:1 split (denominator 3, faster ISP taking remainders 0 and 1) landed at 65.5% vs 34.5%, close to the 66.7/33.3 target. A single 10 GB file over one 50 Mbps connection still takes 26.7 minutes — two ISPs don't make one download twice as fast.

Common mistakes: check-gateway pointed only at the modem (route stays up while the internet behind it is dead), both-addresses-and-ports splitting one server's connections across two public IPs (sites that bind sessions to an IP log users out), and PCC on Hotspot (the docs say it doesn't fit, since Hotspot's web proxy only uses the default table). FastTrack doesn't break PCC on RouterOS 7.25.1 in CHR testing.

The full Indonesian guide with complete CLI blocks is on my blog: https://www.miftahulrizki.eu.org/2026/11/load-balancing-pcc-di-mikrotik-cara.html — it walks through the mangle rules, weighted PCC for unequal ISP speeds, and how to verify with /ip firewall mangle print stats.

Brain implant technology startup Science Corp. raises $400M to bring its medical devices to market

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Brain-computer interface company Science Corp. today revealed it has raised $400 million in a Series D round of funding that brings its valuation soaring to $3.5 billion.

The round, announced in a blog post earlier today, was led by high-profile venture capital firm Khosla Ventures. It was joined by the Ackman Oxman Institute, Tenet Healthcare, 885 Capital, Y Combinator and PLC Neurotech. The Ackman Oxman Institute’s participation is notable, as it appears to be one of brain research center’s first ventures after being established by the hedge fund billionaire Bill Ackman and his wife Neri Oxman earlier this year.

The money from today’s round will give Science the capital it needs to bring its flagship product PRIMA to the U.S. healthcare market, having already established traction in Europe. PRIMA is a specialized chip that’s implanted into the eyes of patients suffering from age-related macular degeneration or AMD, which is one of the most common causes of vision loss among older adults. It’s paired with a special pair of glasses that help to restore partial vision to AMD sufferers, and it has already been approved by some European countries.

For instance, PRIMA has already been implanted in the eyes of several patients in Germany, and the company has dozens of additional surgical procedures scheduled to help more patients restore their vision. In addition, the device is expected to be made available in Italy, France and the U.K. in the coming months. Science wants to offer PRIMA to patients who are willing to pay for it out of pocket at a special unit at Moorfields Private Eye Hospital in London, but it has not disclosed how much people would have to pay.

As for the U.S., PRIMA is currently being vetted for its suitability by the Food and Drug Administration. Science founder and Chief Executive Max Hodak, who previously served as the CEO of Elon Musk’s Neuralink Inc., told Bloomberg in an interview today that he’s hoping it will be made available to U.S. patients early next year. That’s because PRIMA is being pushed through the FDA’s Humanitarian Device Exemption pathway, which allows for the rapid approval of experimental medical technologies that can treat rare conditions. While AMD is not uncommon, the company has argued that it’s rare to see the condition affect people in both eyes.

In future, the company also plans to make PRIMA available as a treatment for Stargardt’s disease and retinitis pigmentosa, which also cause blindness.

The PRIMA device consists of 378 pixels that are each equipped with a tiny electrode. When infrared light hits a pixel, it activates the attached electrode, which in turn simulates cells in the patient’s retina. The electrodes thereby substitute retinal tissue lost to disease.

The infrared light that activates the implant’s pixels comes from a pair of glasses worn by the patient. According to Science, the glasses generate infrared signals based on footage from a forward-facing camera. The camera, which records the user’s surroundings, includes a lens that filters interference such as strong sunlight.

While Science is focused on PRIMA for now, its long-term vision is not limited to restoring people’s sight. It’s also developing the Biohybrid implant, which connects directly to the brain. It’s built from cultured neurons that are combined with silicon chips and integrated with the brain’s circuits. The company says it has already identified the first disease it intends to treat with Biohybrid, but has not disclosed what that condition is.

For now, it’s focused on testing Biohybrid. So far, it has only conducted tests on animals, but it hopes to launch its first human clinical trials within the next 16 months. To do this, it has recruited the respected Yale neurosurgery professor Murat Günel, who has previously said that the device holds promise for treating Parkinson’s disease and other brain illnesses and injuries.

More Evidence Sydney Sweeney Might Be Following In Prince Harry And Meghan Markle’s Footsteps And Planning A Move

Going home for the holidays can get complicated.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Prince Harry, Meghan Markle and their two kids, Archie and Lilibet, have been adapting to a completely new life in the U.K. these past few months. However, some recent reports claim the family may already be getting ready to head back to Southern California to spend Christmas this year. That leads to the question: Why aren’t they allegedly starting new traditions across the pond?

The Royal Family famously spends their Christmas holiday at the Sandringham Estate in Norfolk. However, while Prince Harry may have grown up doing Christmas at the countryside property, he and Meghan only spent two holidays there – a month after they got engaged in 2017 and their first Christmas as a married couple in 2018. New claims by InStyle speculate that since they broke the tradition eight years ago, they aren't expecting this year to be any different despite the geographical convenience.

Prince Harry and Meghan Markle spent their first two Christmas holidays with the Royal Family at Sandringham Estate. When that time of year rolled around in 2019, they had already formally separated from the Royal Family and appeared in a candid documentary interview where Harry said he and Prince Williams were "on different paths." So in 2019, they decided to spend the holidays privately in Canada with a nine-month-old Archie. And, in March 2020, they stepped away from being senior royals.

It’s expected that Harry and Meghan will share a more intimate holiday season with Meghan’s mother, Doria Ragland, as they usually do. While it’s been suggested they’d likely end the year in the U.S. (via People), a source with the Daily Mail reports Doria may be the one to head to the U.K. instead. Nothing’s been confirmed, but another reason why they aren’t going to the Sandringham Estate could be because they are simply not invited, per an October report:

They know it would be too awkward and too difficult [to spend Christmas with the royal family]. They also know they’re not going to be invited, and they’re not waiting for an invitation.

It's also worth taking into account that Meghan Markle and her mother have been reportedly leaning on each other following Markle’s father facing allegations of possessing child sexual abuse material. Thomas Markle Sr. was arrested by federal agents on November 6 and could serve prison time.

Over in the U.K., the couple reportedly haven’t found their forever home yet since relocating in August. They are currently living at a friend’s private estate in the Cotswolds. Perhaps they are searching for familiarity this holiday season after the big move?

In a 2025 email sent to followers of her lifestyle brand, As Ever, Markle said she was planning on spending the holiday that year with “pajamas all day, some Scrabble or Sequence (or Candyland for the kids), music playing, candles flickering, dogs snoring, endless grazing; the laughter, the sidebars, the deep breaths (you know the ones), the fun, and the memories.” The Royal Family’s Christmas traditions, on the other hand, involve a black-tie dinner and attending service at St Mary Magdalene Church on Christmas morning.

Markle has shared her love of Christmas before, calling it her favorite holiday and even making a special called With Love, Meghan: Holiday Celebration, which you can stream now with a Netflix subscription. Juggling the holidays is rarely easy, even for a power couple like those two.

Marco Rubio is adamant the various outages plaguing the U.S. this week aren't related.

During a press conference Tuesday, the Secretary of State shut down speculation that all the outages are linked ... saying there's no evidence to back up the theory.

He also stressed that they're not the result of "nefarious actors" or a cyberattack, saying again that there's no evidence or reason to believe that's the case.

As you know, the U.S. has been experiencing a slew of outages for the past several days.

Last week, Microsoft went down just one day before the FAA issued a rare ground stop that the government said is the result of an equipment issue.

After that, several major banks -- Chase, Wells Fargo, Bank of America and U.S. Bank -- all experienced various levels of outages.

Next to go dark was the NSA's classified communication network and the National Weather Service.

But according to Rubio it's all just a crazy coincidence, apparently.

Nvidia’s startup stakes ballooned to $65B, but Huang’s exit puts future deals in doubt

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia’s equity in private companies continues to grow: it revealed its stakes are now worth $65.5 billion on Tuesday, up from the $22 billion reported in January. The increase includes both new investments and significant markups of existing portfolio companies.

The company invested $16 billion into equities in the past quarter alone, it said, and $58 billion in the past nine months of its fiscal year. This amount includes buying shares in public and private companies, yet it shows how the chipmaker made massive bets over the course of the year, especially compared to last year; in November 2025, the company reported spending $5 billion buying equity in the first nine months of that fiscal year.

But it’s unclear if this growth will continue under the interim CEO and Jensen Huang’s successor.

Nvidia stated in late 2023 that the goal of its corporate investing is to expand the AI ecosystem by backing startups it considers to be “game changers and market makers.”  Over the nearly three years that followed, the chipmaker has invested in nearly 350 startups, according to PitchBook. The companies Nvidia backed include Anthropic, OpenAI; neolabs like Thinking Machines and Reflection; neoclouds, like Lambda, Nscale, and Crusoe; as well as other companies developing AI infrastructure, robotics startups, and applications.

It’s also become a big financier of the data center build out, reporting $118 billion in guarantees with $105 billion of that the backstop it supplied for OpenAI’s $500 billion data center in Ohio.

The company made those commitments by allocating a meaningful portion of its excess cash flows. In Tuesday’s earning call, Kress said Nvidia would continue providing financing assistance to strong customers and that “as the market matures and additional sources of capital become available,” the company would be “rigorously evaluating future investments, commitments, and guarantees.”

But the question for the VC ecosystem is whether the company will continue to back startups at anywhere near the same level without Huang at the helm.

Huang’s rampant dealmaking has reportedly been a source of concern for Kress. Investors have told TechCrunch that they anticipate that she will take a more conservative approach to startup investing, becoming more selective with new investments and writing smaller-sized checks into its existing portfolio companies.

“We intend to return all excess free cash flow net of strategic uses to shareholders during this last quarter and going forward,” Kress said on the earnings call on Tuesday. Just how strategic startups will be under her leadership, and under the future permanent CEO, remains to be seen. But for now, VCs are bracing for a meaningful pullback in capital.

‘Grand Theft Auto VI’ Revs Its Engines & Leads 2026 Game Awards Nominations: Full List

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fans may cast their votes now through Dec. 9.

Grand Theft Auto VI is one of the year’s most-anticipated video games, even ahead of its official release on Thursday (Nov. 19). Now, it’s the leading contender for the 2026 Game Awards, which honor the video game industry’s most innovative games, studios and creatives.

The Rockstar Games title received eight nominations, including game of the year, best score & music, best action/adventure and best narrative.

This year’s list of nominees includes 112 different games, individuals and teams across a variety of gaming genres and platforms.

This year’s other game of the year nominees are 007 First Light from IO Interactive; Control Resonant from Remedy and Annapurna; Phantom Blade Zero from S-GAME; Resident Evil Requiem from Capcom; and The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time from Nintendo.

The other nominees for best score & music, presented in association with Spotify, are Ace Combat 8: Wings of Theve (Bandai Namco Aces/Bandai Namco), Mixtape (Beethoven & Dinosaur/Annapurna), Phantom Blade Zero (S-GAME) and The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time (Nintendo).

Nintendo is the most nominated publisher with 12 nods across its combined portfolio, followed by Capcom with 11 nods, and Annapurna Interactive and PlayStation with nine nods each.

Nominees are selected by a jury of over 160 media publications and creator outlets from around the world. From now through Dec. 9 at 6 p.m. PT, fans will be able to help decide the winners in all categories via online voting on TheGameAwards.com. In China, fans can vote for their favorites on a variety of platforms including Bilibili.

The Game Awards will air live from the Peacock Theater in Los Angeles on Thursday, Dec. 10. Lorne Balfe will conduct The Game Awards Orchestra. Balfe, a top scorer for film and TV, has also scored such video games as Assassin’s Creed Revelations, Assassin’s Creed III, Crysis 2, Skylanders and Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 2. Since the 2018 Game Awards, he has conducted a medley of the themes of the six nominees for game of the year to accompany the presentation of the award at the end of the show.

This year’s show will stream for free across more than 40 digital video platforms including Disney+, Live TV on PS5, Prime Video, Roblox, Steam, TikTok Live, Twitch, X and YouTube. The Game Awards 2025 broke viewership records with an estimated 171 million global livestreams, up 11% over 2024’s showcase.

The Game Awards is once again partnering with Twitch for “Twitch Predicts The Game Awards,” an extension where participants can assemble a bracket of the titles they anticipate will win big at The Game Awards.

The Game Awards is executive produced by Geoff Keighley and Kimmie Kim. Richard Preuss is the director, LeRoy Bennett is the creative director, and Lara Karchmer is co-executive producer.

Researchers train AI in simulation and unlock real robot skills

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Researchers trained a 27-billion-parameter AI model in simulation to control an unfamiliar robot, succeeding in 60% of real-world trials.

Researchers behind PEAR, a system for training AI models to control robots, reported that a model completed 18 of 30 physical trials after learning its robot skills entirely in simulation.

The preprint submitted Nov. 13 describes robot post-training of Qwen3.8-27B, an open-weight reasoning model with 27 billion parameters. Researchers used reinforcement learning without demonstrations during that stage.

The result offers evidence that simulated practice can teach skills that carry over to different physical hardware. The final model had already practiced the two evaluated task types, pickup and block alignment, in simulation.

The physical test used a dual-arm YAM robot, which none of the compared models received training on in this study. It included 20 pickup attempts across ten objects and ten alignment attempts across five block layouts, with each object or layout tried twice.

The final model succeeded in 12 pickup attempts and six alignment attempts. A checkpoint trained only on the LIBERO simulation benchmark completed seven of the 30 trials.

The final model had continued training on the corresponding two tasks in RoboDojo using a different robot, according to the paper's physical-trial account.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

PEAR researchers report 18 successes in 30 physical trials after robot post-training in simulation. Explore how the tested checkpoints compare.

Base model: 0 of 30 physical trials succeeded. No robot post-training in this study.

LIBERO-only checkpoint: 7 of 30 physical trials succeeded. Robot training in LIBERO simulation.

Final trained model: 18 of 30 physical trials succeeded. LIBERO, then two RoboDojo task types.

Two practiced task types transferred to a different physical robot; the final model completed 18 of 30 trials.

Author-reported preprint, November 13, 2026; not independent replication. The final model practiced both evaluated task types in simulation. No real-robot training data were used in this study; simulation-only describes robot post-training, not foundation training. Prompt dependence and long reasoning remain limits.

Schematic training-to-evaluation path for the final model. Each dot represents one physical trial, grouped by aggregate outcome rather than actual trial order. Reported totals are 0/30, 7/30 and 18/30; no new rates or per-task checkpoint counts are derived.

Author-reported preprint, November 13, 2026; not independent replication. The final model practiced both evaluated task types in simulation. No real-robot training data were used in this study; simulation-only describes robot post-training, not foundation training. Prompt dependence and long reasoning remain limits.

At each turn, the model reads camera images and the robot's state, reasons in text, and specifies changes to the gripper's pose. Inverse kinematics translates the target pose into joint movements, which a controller executes.

The prompt supplies coordinate frames, gripper orientation, cameras, and task-completion conditions, and the authors acknowledge that performance depends on those descriptions.

All three models used the same high reasoning-effort setting, with trials having a 25-round budget and failing if a single round exceeded 32,000 reasoning tokens. The final model's successful attempts required 7 to 24 rounds.

The authors say reasoning before every move slows tasks. Their separate decoding demonstration reached 747 tokens per second on a GB300 using lower-precision NVFP4 weights and DFlash2, a decoding accelerator.

That suggests about 4.3 seconds to generate an average high-effort reasoning round. The reported evaluations used BF16 weights because quantization reduced accuracy, so the speed demonstration and physical success rate describe different operating conditions.

The public PEAR repository provides training infrastructure and deployment recipes. The result remains an author-reported experiment pending independent replication, and turning it into a broadly useful robot system will require wider task coverage and shorter reasoning delays.

Why Similarweb Stock Surged Today

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The intelligence platform provides its customers with a comprehensive view of the digital economy.

Shares of Similarweb (SMWB +13.04%) climbed 13% on Tuesday after the data analytics provider reported strong gains in sales and profits.

The software company's revenue rose 16% to $83 million in the third quarter.

Similarweb acts as a digital intelligence supplier to over 10,000 organizations around the world. Its ever-expanding data set spans more than 100 million websites, 4 million apps, and 235 million e-commerce products.

Demand for Similarweb's insights is rising as competition within the digital economy intensifies. Customers generating annual recurring revenue (ARR) of $100,000 or more grew 6% to 474.

These customers are also spending more on Similarweb's platform. Its dollar-based net retention rate (NRR) for clients with ARR of at least $100,000 increased to 114% from 105% in the prior-year period.

Notably, artificial intelligence (AI)-powered offerings are helping to accelerate Similarweb's growth. AI-related business accounted for approximately two-thirds of ARR added in the third quarter, according to chief financial officer Ran Vered.

Better still, Similarweb is growing more profitable as it scales its operations. Adjusted operating margin improved to 12% from 6% in the year-ago quarter. Adjusted net income, in turn, more than doubled to $9.4 million, or $0.10 per share.

Management now expects full-year revenue to grow by roughly 13% to $319 million in 2026, resulting in adjusted operating profit of about $30 million.

"As AI takes on more of the analysis and action, the quality, breadth, and the freshness of the underlying data matter more, and that is where we remain special," co-founder Or Offer said during a conference call with analysts.

FAA official oversaw router work just before nationwide ground stop, report claims

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An official with the U.S. Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) was on the ground in Arkansas when new telecommunications wires were replaced, an action that preceded Friday's national ground stop by eight minutes, according to the Washington Post.

The FAA official oversaw the work, tested it, and signed off on it, according to the Post's report. The work involved replacing a router and swapping old copper wires for new fiber-optic cables, part of a national effort by the FAA to upgrade the airline industry's communication infrastructure.

The fact that an FAA official greenlit the work complicates Transportation Secretary Sean Duffy's efforts to point fingers at L3Harris Technologies, a tech services provider and contractor tasked with updating the wires in Arkansas and leading the FAA's fiber-optic upgrades. Duffy said on Saturday that his agency is looking into whether "incompetence" by L3Harris led to the 4.5-hour ground stop.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Responding to the Post's request for comment, the FAA pointed to statements Duffy made on Tuesday in a news conference at the Capitol. During the conference, Duffy said that he wanted L3Harris to "hire better minds" for the wire replacement work and was considering bringing in new contractors to help.

An exact cause for the communications outage has not yet been confirmed, an anonymous source told the Post. Duffy told reporters on Saturday that the replaced router in Arkansas was faulty and flooded the network with over 1,000 connection attempts every few seconds, overwhelming it and making it difficult for planes in the air to communicate with air traffic controllers.

The FAA and L3Harris officials are currently studying the Arkansas router, according to the Post. That device was known as a Juniper router, the Post reported, and L3Harris has stopped installing them, though dozens have already been put in service.

A Renewed Humanism

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 17, 2026 | Reviewed by Monica Vilhauer Ph.D.

Pope Leo’s Magnifica Humanitas (2026), subtitled “On Safeguarding the Human Person in the Time of Artificial Intelligence,” has been heralded as one of the most significant and morally serious visions for how humanity ought to think about and navigate the advent of the AI era. Acknowledging “the ambiguity of tools,” which are neither morally good nor bad in and of themselves but “can cause harm when not oriented toward the good,” the Pope insists that the end toward which our aspirations aim is as important as the aspirations themselves.

For Leo, protecting and fostering the dignity of the human person must be the goal of everything we do. Contrasting two images taken from Scripture, the Pope calls us to consider how the desire that orients our ambitions changes the things we create.

In Leo’s first example, the construction of the Tower of Babel (Genesis 11:1–9) is motivated by fear and vainglory:

Fearing being scattered across the earth, they [the builders] sought to guarantee stability and power for themselves, and above all to ‘make a name’ for themselves.” Such motives cannot but end in ruin. Leo continues, “When a city is built on pride and the claim to self-sufficiency, communication breaks down, languages are confused and people no longer understand each other. The result is not unity, but dispersion. Babel thus reveals the limits of any effort that, however grandiose, arises from self-affirmation, sacrifices human dignity for efficiency and aspires to reach heaven without God’s blessing.

In his second, which comes from the Book of Nehemiah, Nehemiah returns to the city of Jerusalem after the Babylonian exile only to find it in ruins. Heartbroken, he takes in the destruction in silence. Then, before setting to work, he “convened the families, assigned each of them a section of the wall to rebuild, listened to their concerns, coordinated their efforts and addressed any opposition.”

Nehemiah, that is, kept the dignity of those around him at the center of his project rather than running headlong toward apparent solutions. In so doing, he was able to resurrect what had been lost while simultaneously creating something new. The Pope writes:

The narrative shows how the city is reborn, not through the initiative of one man, but through the shared responsibility of all: men, women, priests, artisans, heads of households and young people all play a part. It is an undertaking with God at the center, which rebuilds relationships before rebuilding with stones. Thus, ancient Jerusalem rediscovers a common language—not one of uniformity, but one of communion, namely the harmony that arises when all persons assume their own role and recognize that their strength comes from the Lord.

What makes these images apt for our current moment—and what many commentators have overlooked—is the role language plays in each. For, if the desire of those constructing the Tower of Babel was to create a single, unified language with which all could understand and be understood, so too is the aim of Artificial Intelligence to provide a kind of totalizing language capable of flattening ambiguity and rendering difference obsolete. And if Nehemiah’s success was rooted in the fact that he sought out the input of those he hoped to serve, and even (perhaps especially) those with whom he disagreed, so too must dialogue be at the foundation of technological advance.

Consider another example, this one taken from contemporary fiction, that draws out this tension inherent in language—the tension between the desire to communicate directly, if only superficially, and the desire to express how fraught and complex human experience actually is, even if that expression can only be accomplished indirectly and ambiguously.

It comes from Paul Auster’s great work of metareal fiction City of Glass (1987), in which the story’s chief antagonist Peter Stillman—is he still a man, still human, or has he been deformed by his mad pursuit of “perfectibility”?—obsesses over the duplicity of language, the way one word can mean two equal and opposite things. For instance, the word cleave “means both ‘to join together’ and ‘to break apart.’”

For Stillman, the Genesis narrative to which the Pope alluded “records not only the fall of man, but the fall of language” from clarity to opacity. Once, he conjectures, language was simple, direct, and stated exactly what it meant to say without confusion or ambiguity. There was a time when “words had not been merely appended to things . . . they revealed their essences . . . A thing and its name were interchangeable.” But after the destruction of the Tower of Babel, this was no longer true. Now words conceal as much as they reveal, and deception is built into the very nature of how we communicate.

Driven mad by the desire to use language to reunify what appears to him to be a fractured and fragmentary existence, Stillman sets out to realize a utopian future in which his model of a single, unified language makes absolute knowledge and mastery possible. (Sound familiar?)

If the fall of man also entailed a fall of language, was it not logical to assume that it would be possible to undo the fall, to reverse its effects by undoing the fall of language? . . . If man could learn to speak this original language of innocence, did it not follow that he would thereby recover a state of innocence within himself?

The trouble is that Stillman is not able to conjure up this mythical language model, and his attempts to do so inflict immense suffering on those around him.

In an ironic twist, Stillman ultimately resorts to deception himself in a vain attempt to accomplish his goal. Creating a character named Henry Dark, a fictional disciple of John Milton, he attributes his most controversial ideas to Dark and even falsifies the historical record to make it appear as though Dark had heralded his, Stillman’s, project over 300 years in advance.

What could offer a better image of the LLMs that seem so powerful and omniscient today—with their false citations and hallucinated facts—than a fictional character invented by a mad genius to dupe others into believing that his language model could solve the mysteries and riddles of the human condition? And yet, what could offer a better reason to hope? For, although Dark was invented by Stillman to serve darker ends, still his creation was—as Pope Leo recently called the craft of writing fiction—a profound “act of humanity,” one which points to the supreme dignity of its author even if he, like many of us today, is too consumed with his utopian ideas to appreciate it.

Auster, P. (1987). City of Glass. New York, NY: Penguin Books.

Leo XIV. (2026). Magnifica Humanitas: On Safeguarding the Human Person in the Time of Artificial Intelligence. Encyclical letter. The Holy See.

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Last of Us cast will be saying goodbye to each other and the Clickers soon.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Last of Us is a TV show that has meant a lot to a lot of people, as it has successfully translated the emotional core of the hugely popular video game and built on the complexity of the character relationships. The final season, anticipated to debut in 2027, is sure to be emotional for fans, too. So it’s not at all surprising to hear that the cast is emotional as they get to the end of filming and will have to say goodbye to each other.

An HBO show that got the world talking from the moment it debuted, the post-apocalyptic drama started by following Joel (Pedro Pascal) as he escorted the teenage Ellie (Bella Ramsey) across a devastated United States ravaged by a fungal infection that had turned people into violent creatures. With Season 3 delving deeper into the journey of Joel’s brother, Tommy Miller, actor Gabriel Luna not only has to say goodbye to a character he’ll know much better, but he’ll also be leaving the cast and crew behind after bidding them an emotional farewell. Here’s what he told Entertainment Weekly about the emotions that are already starting to swirl:

Oh man, a lot of emotions are going around. But right now I think we’re all too tired to feel them. [Laughs.] Because it’s just massive. Obviously I can’t give anything away, but people who’ve played the game know the level of the scope of what we’re dealing with here towards the end of the story. I’m so proud of Bella [Ramsey], Kaitlyn [Dever], and Kyriana [Kratter], who’s joined the cast, for being the tip of the spear and shouldering the weight of this production and what it requires from them.

If you played The Last of Us games, you knew that Abby would be seen on the show as a villain after killing Pedro Pascal's Joel. No matter her motivation or the extent of her own trauma, it would be challenging for viewers to understand the actions of someone taking out such a beloved character. And then, the Season 2 finale ended with the focus shifting to Kaitlyn Dever's Abby, who will take center stage in The Last of Us Season 3.

In a world like the one portrayed in The Last of Us, where everything is heightened as you just try to survive and not get killed or infected, emotions understandably run high for the cast helping bring that world to life. And even though Tommy comes in and out of the story, The Terminal List actor can’t help but be impressed by how the Ellie, Abby, and Lev actors are handling everything required of them, as they head closer to the finish line. He went on to say:

The bulk of my work was back in the summer, and then I’d go back and forth and come in for two, three weeks of time, but they’re here every day and doing the lion’s share of the work.

Since the Naughty Dog video game-turned-TV series is a massive production, it sounds like there’s going to be a prolonged series of goodbyes taking place as storylines are wrapped up and actors depart. Gabriel Luna not only has to process the emotion of saying goodbye to his own character and wrapping up a story that he likely feels very connected to after three seasons, but he also has to handle the departure of each of his co-stars. That’s a lot of emotions for the Dexter: Resurrection star, who will probably be exhausted by the time it’s all said and done. He pretty much said just that:

We’re getting to the end of it, and people are starting to wrap, and we’re giving them their flowers, and then they’re heading off, and one by one, the family is dwindling down to just the final few. We’re about to get a welcomed break and respite from what we’ve been doing, and then we have to come back and finish strong after the holiday. We’re so excited for this thing to come out.

I can only imagine the tears that will be flowing and the emotions that will be felt as the final days of the HBO series come to an end, and everyone says their final goodbyes. Fans anxiously anticipating the third season will have to pack their patience pants while waiting for details on when it will officially premiere. In the meantime, the first two seasons of The Last of Us are streaming with an HBO Max subscription.

GTA 6's Release Will Stress The Internet Just As Everything's Falling Apart

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rockstar's game arrives amid major network outages

In early November, some internet service providers warned their customers that pre-downloads of Grand Theft Auto 6 were causing network slowdown issues for everyone on the service. That problem became visible before millions of gamers tried to log into the same game simultaneously. A long list of recent outages and issues across industries reliant on tech and network services now makes it seem likely that the biggest game launch of all time won't go down smoothly.

If you've ever been around for the release of a big new game, you know that day-one network issues are fairly common. Some hiccups are worse than others, of course, but most people are probably already bracing for some potential hitches when we're talking about the biggest game launch of all time. What makes GTA 6 different from other game releases is its scale and timing.

The highly anticipated sequel first began causing issues for some ISPs when Rockstar opened preloads for the 212 GB game on Nov. 10. At the time, some providers claimed that the game was directly responsible for never-before-seen traffic peaks so high that they congested the proverbial internet tubes for everyone else.

Rockstar undoubtedly foresaw that the problem could occur or worsen by launch day. The studio dropped a couple of patches in a staggered rollout prior to launch. The release cadence made it less likely that GTA 6 fans could overload Rockstar's, PlayStation's, and Xbox's servers at the same time. Service providers have also been gearing up for Nov. 19, with at least one ISP advertising that it would offer increased download speeds specifically for GTA 6.

Yet network issues of some sort, both in the game and the internet at large, may be unavoidable on Thursday. Recent trends show that many online services are already hanging on by a thread. Earlier in November, flights across the country were halted due to apparent issues with aging routers that could not handle the amount of data shared by newer pieces of equipment. During that same week, Microsoft products like Outlook and Excel went dark for nearly 24 hours because a faulty gateway was overwhelmed by the amount of data passing through it. On Nov. 16, customers were unable to access accounts at major banks like Chase or through payment services like Zelle.

On Nov. 17, Apple users reported widespread issues with iCloud, AppleCare, App Store, and Apple Music. Apple has not revealed the cause of the issues, noting only that outages are slowing down services. On that same Tuesday, news broke that the United States' national security network also went down after weeks of wonkiness.

The quick succession of these instances has led to conspiracy theories online that they're the result of cyberattacks. So far, there's no evidence to support such suspicions in relation to recent newsworthy outages. It's probable that the specific causes of all of these events vary widely, but there's an overarching theme among all of them. Online services, by and large, are built on fragile interconnected systems. Some of those systems rely on old hardware and software that are buckling under normal but increasing strain. Even modernizing systems are not immune — those efforts may actually be responsible for the sudden tech difficulties.

Kris Beevers, CEO of a network and infrastructure company, told the BBC on Monday, “I expect we’ll see more meaningful outages in the infrastructure that essential parts of society depend on in coming months as the invisible infrastructure that our world depends on becomes more strained and visible.”

Since GTA 6 is already responsible for an internet-wide slowdown by some ISPs, it may not be a bad idea to preload the game and its updates ASAP. The same advice applies to those who have purchased physical copies — the game has to be downloaded from Rockstar. Nov. 19 will still probably be rocky for the game if not for the entire internet. But hey: if GTA 6 took over a decade to get here, what's another day?

New GTA 6 gameplay images show fans random world events that impact the entire environment

General-purpose robot maker Genesis AI reels in $500M at $3.5B valuation

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Genesis AI SAS today announced that it has raised $500 million in funding from a group of prominent investors.

Premji Invest, Khosla Ventures and Eclipse led the Series A round. They were joined by the venture capital arms of Nvidia Corp., Advanced Micro Devices Inc., Zoom Communications Inc., Samsung Electronics Co. and Ericsson. Former Google Chief Executive Eric Schmidt contributed as well along with other tech executives.

Paris-based Genesis develops a general-purpose robot called Eno (pictured). Unlike humanoid robots, it moves using a wheeled base rather than legs. The wheeled base can adjust Eno’s height using a mechanism that consists of two large panels connected by a hinge. The module enables the robot to adapt its configuration to each task.

Eno is powered by a custom artificial intelligence model called GENE. Genesis says that the algorithm is easier to train than traditional robotics-optimized neural networks, which can translate to higher output quality.

The dataset on which an AI model is trained must be tailored to the robot it’s intended to control. If a company is building an AI model for a four-legged robot, the training dataset must include footage of four-legged robots. The more specialized the robot, the more difficult it is to collect the necessary training data.

Genesis has come up with a workaround to that challenge. Eno’s palms and fingers are based on a custom design closely modeled after the human hand. As a result, the robot can be trained on demonstrations in which humans perform tasks with their hands. That kind of training data is much easier to produce than footage of robots performing work.

Eno’s robotic hands have 20 degrees of freedom, which means they can perform that many motions. The human hand has 27 degrees of freedom. Additionally, Genesis covered the hands with a soft material designed to mimic skin.

The GENE foundation model that powers Emo can perform tasks comprising dozens of steps. Genesis has released demo videos that show the system playing the piano, cooking and solving a Rubik’s Cube. According the company, that versatility enables Eno to operate in not only industrial environments but also homes.

“We’re working to build the world’s best general-purpose robot,” said Genesis co-founder and CEO Zhou Xian. “By building it all ourselves, we can obsess over every detail and improve the entire robot faster in one closed loop. This funding will accelerate our efforts to deploy our robots in the real world at scale.”

The company will use the capital to hire more AI researchers and roboticists. Additionally, Genesis will step up its training data collection efforts in a bid to expand its AI model’s capabilities.

Nvidia says it won't reinstate Huang as firm reports bumper results

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang and Nvidia's interim CEO, Colette Kress

Nvidia's quarterly sales doubled to top $100bn for the first time as the company insisted it has no plans to reinstate ousted co-founder Jensen Huang.

In the company's first results since Huang's dramatic exit, interim chief executive Colette Kress said it is "business as usual" and told investors: "We've got this."

The company's quarterly sales doubled from a year earlier, reaching $115.8bn (Â£86.9bn), with shares in the company jumping by 4.8% in after-hours trading.

Earlier on Tuesday, Huang said he would be "honoured" to return and lead Nvidia, having quit following alleged extramarital affairs, including one with a suspected spy for China.

"The board does not intend to reinstate him," Nvidia chair Harvey Jones said during a call on Tuesday with financial analysts.

The company's profits also doubled from a year ago. For the quarter, which covered the three months to 25 October, profit hit $65bn. Its revenue from data centres, a significant part of Nvidia's business, also doubled to $107bn in the same period.

Going forward, the company said its rapid growth is poised to continue, and revenue will rise by another 13% in the next quarter.

Watch: Who is suspected Chinese spy at centre of Nvidia affair investigation?

Given the uncertainty surrounding the company after Huang's exit, financial analysts had been unsure that Nvidia would provide any forward-looking guidance.

Daniela Hathorn, a senior analyst for trading platform Capital.com, said Nvidia's results "offer a strong answer to concerns that demand for AI infrastructure is cooling" and temper immediate worries that Huang was irreplaceable as a leader.

"The harder question is whether Nvidia can preserve the strategic advantage Huang helped build," Hathorn added. "His exit is unlikely to change orders already committed, but could matter more when Nvidia makes its next round of decisions on products, partnerships and investment."

Kress seemed eager to stress that Nvidia can continue to not only succeed, but lead the ongoing AI buildout that is happening throughout the technology industry.

"It is business as usual," she said. "Our partners, our suppliers, all of them are 100% behind us."

She added that Nvidia's leadership team has "stood up very strongly over the last week... working seamlessly in all of their departments and projects".

"Our momentum is real. Our demand is accelerating. Our supply is growing. Our customers and our partners are with us, and employees are committed." Kress said before leaving the analyst call. "We've got this."

The interim CEO also said Nvidia is no longer shipping Hopper data centre semiconductors to China.

The Hopper technology is in Nvidia's advanced H200 processors, which the firm won approval to sell in China after Huang's repeated lobbying efforts in Washington.

They had previously been restricted over concerns that selling them to China would give its industries and military an edge over the US.

Kress did not elaborate on why the shipments had been stopped.

The rise and fall of Jensen Huang - how the tech superstar became mired in scandal

Jensen Huang says he would be 'honoured' to return as Nvidia CEO if board asked him

Despite board chairman Jones saying earlier that the company would not answer analysts' questions about Huang or Nvidia's investigation into his conduct, he did address the search for a permanent replacement.

He said Nvidia is working with an executive search firm and is considering internal and external candidates, but that the company would not rush its selection.

"With the advanced technology and the national security dynamics in the marketplace, our new CEO will have to navigate those waters carefully," Jones said. "The search will be thorough and we want to do it as quickly as possible, but we won't do it before we find the exact right person."

Michael Monaghan from investment firm Founder ETFs is among those who want Huang back in the top job at Nvidia.

The Nvidia investor told the BBC that he is waiting to see the results of a thorough investigation into Huang's actions, instead of relying on "salacious allegations" made in the media.

Nvidia's strong earnings are "from the machine that Jensen built" and a company tends to lose "its shine and vision" after an iconic founder leaves, Monaghan said.
