Google Wallet is launching on iOS

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In case you, for some reason, don’t want to use the nearly identical Apple Wallet.

Google and Apple have a long history of not launching first-party software for their rival's hardware, so it's usually a bit of a surprise when these companies manage to make nice. The latest instance is Google Wallet, which is available starting today on iOS for US users.

It's an odd integration considering Apple has its own Wallet app that's very similar to Google's offering. Both can handle digital payment tools, they store tickets and travel information and they safely store IDs and credentials.

The notable standout in today's announcement is the positioning of Google Wallet as a hub for agentic purchases made across Google tools. That seems like the only area where this could be a markedly different app experience, since Apple has been more cautious about agentic AI than its competitors to date. The iOS version of Google Wallet also will not support tap-to-pay, but the blog post didn't specify whether that feature might be added in the future.

D-Link DIR-806A Rev A1 Login - Default Gateway and Credentials

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The D-Link DIR-806A Rev A1 is an AC750 dual-band router — 2.4 GHz and 5 GHz in one box — and like most D-Link hardware it hides its settings behind a small web page at a fixed address. If you need to change the Wi-Fi password, check who is connected or update the firmware, the login below is the way in.

The full model guide, with the steps and fixes in order, is on WiFi Router Zone.

Once you are in, the useful pages are the wireless settings, where you change the network name and Wi-Fi password on each band; the DHCP client list, which shows every device on your network; and the management or firmware section, where you check for updates and reboot the router. Change the Wi-Fi password first if the router is still on its factory settings, and write the new password down before you save it.

The page never loads. Make sure you are connected to the DIR-806A itself, not mobile data or another network, and check the address — D-Link uses 192.168.0.1, not 192.168.1.1. On Windows, ipconfig shows your real gateway on the Default Gateway line. Note that dlinkrouter.local only works while you are connected to the D-Link router. The same check-the-gateway-first habit applies to every brand — see our Hathway router login guide for another worked example.

The login is rejected. Passwords are case-sensitive, so check Caps Lock. If the admin password was changed and forgotten, press and hold the recessed reset button for about 10 seconds, wait for the router to restart, then sign in again with admin / admin and run the setup wizard.

Still stuck? If your unit is a different DIR-806A hardware revision, check the label on the bottom or back of the device — the gateway address and default credentials are printed there, and the label always wins over any guide.

What it costs an AI agent to read 100,000 web pages

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I wanted to know what my agent's web reading actually costs, so I collected the published prices of the scraping, search and browser APIs people use for agents and put them on one per-1,000 scale.

OpenBot: How to Run a Team of AI Agents on Your Computer

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 17 • Originally published at blense.tech

Everyone has been part of a school group project where each member wrote their section alone at home without talking to anyone else. The night before the deadline, someone pasted everything into a single document. The result was an academic Frankenstein: the introduction ignored the conclusion, every section cited sources differently, and arguments contradicted each other right on the page.

Despite rapid progress in language models, most of our day-to-day work with AI still repeats that exact dynamic. We open a chat window, ask a question, copy the response, and close the tab. The next second, all project context and reasoning vanish.

It feels like hiring a brilliant freelancer with severe amnesia: they have to relearn your entire business from scratch before every single task.

For one-off questions, that transactional relationship works fine. For ongoing workflows—code maintenance, product launches, support triage, or operational monitoring—the real leverage comes from accumulating context over weeks. That is the shift OpenBot, a source-available project by Synthetify Labs, puts into practice by replacing the disposable chatbot with a resident operational team right on your computer.

OpenBot's core difference isn't launching yet another proprietary model. The desktop app is built on a local-first architecture and follows a Bring Your Own Plan model: instead of charging token markups, it connects to the AI subscriptions and API keys you already pay for—or to local model servers running on your own hardware.

Rather than relying on ephemeral cloud threads, every team member gets a continuous technical environment on your machine built on four pillars:

In practice, the source-available codebase in nightly-labs/openbot (under the PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0 license, free for personal use on macOS, Windows, and Linux, with remote access via web, iOS, and Android) turns AI from an isolated utility into a permanent deskmate.

With context preserved on disk, agents no longer live in solitary tabs—they coexist in shared channels, much like a #launch room, except populated by humans and models from different vendors working side by side.

In the same thread, an agent backed by Codex can investigate a bug, another on Claude Code writes tests or reviews the patch, while a third on Gemini or Grok organizes release notes. The ecosystem also supports OpenCode (including free models with no account required), Cursor, Cline, Muse Code, Pi, Inception's Mercury models, any agent compatible with the Agent Client Protocol (ACP)—such as Goose, Qwen Code, and GitHub Copilot CLI—and 100% local models via Ollama and LM Studio.

Working together in a persistent space unlocks a decisive shift: strict specialization. Instead of a generic assistant doing everything adequately, the official OpenBot Marketplace catalog features 27 agents with narrow scopes and explicit boundaries:

Putting brilliant specialists in the same room creates an obvious risk: without a protocol, everyone talks and edits files at the same time.

To prevent collisions, OpenBot channels run on crash-safe FIFO message queues (First In, First Out) with strict state control. At any given moment, there is only a single task owner.

A real workflow shows how this engine turns without human micromanagement:

An assistant that only acts when you type a command still demands constant mental overhead to remember, prompt, and follow up. To remove that friction, OpenBot attaches scheduled routines directly to resident agents.

Think of them as classic cron jobs, except they wake up the full language model with access to local context, accumulated history, and authenticated browser tabs. Chief of Staff wakes on weekday mornings to cross-check your calendar, emails, and commitments and post a ~25-line briefing before your workday starts. QA Tester runs morning smoke checks against production, while Ops Digest sweeps Notion, Airtable, or Trello boards on Friday afternoons to flag overdue items.

Yet the most mature engineering detail in this architecture is the silence rule, implemented through the routine_no_update function. In immature automations, if nothing broke overnight, the bot still sends a cheerful message saying everything looks fine—flooding your phone with operational noise.

A well-designed routine works like a smart alarm clock: it wakes up on its own, reads your emails, checks your flight status, and only wakes you up if boarding has been canceled.

When an agent runs its routine in OpenBot and finds no anomalies or relevant updates, its instructions tell it to call routine_no_update. The channel stays completely silent, protecting human focus.

Giving background agents access to local directories, a terminal, and an authenticated browser raises an inevitable security question. OpenBot's technical documentation is candid about this: after initial consent, local agents run with danger-full-access permissions and approvalPolicy: never inside the local environment.

Because local analysis and execution are unrestricted to avoid constant permission prompts, containment relies on two concrete barriers:

It is a pragmatic split between analysis autonomy and execution autonomy: your digital team prepares everything behind the scenes, while the bridge to the real world requires a human circuit breaker.

In the original deep dive published on Blense, you'll find the complete comparison table across all 14+ supported runtimes, the agent boundary matrix, architecture diagrams (FIFO queue and human circuit breaker), and remote WebRTC deployment details.

Model ML raises $250M to automate investment banking grunt work

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Model ML, a London-based startup that uses AI to automate work traditionally performed by junior bankers and consultants, including building financial models, preparing pitch decks, and writing due diligence reports, has raised a $250 million in Series B funding at a $1.5 billion valuation. The new round, which comes a year after its $75 million Series A round was announced, was co-led by General Atlantic and earlier backer QED Investors.

Brothers Chaz and Arnie Englander co-founded the three-year-old startup after previously co-founding two other companies, including Fat Llama, a peer-to-peer rental marketplace acquired by Swedish rival Hygglo for $41.5 million in 2022.

Model ML develops AI agents that integrate with clients’ systems, data, and tools, including Excel and PowerPoint. The startup says its agents understand the full context of deals that bankers and consultants are working on, and can automatically update financial models and presentations when new developments affect their analysis.

Other startups automating the routine, repetitive tasks for investment bankers and management consultants include Hebbia and Rogo, which raised $160 million at a $2 billion valuation in April.

Anthropic and OpenAI have also built tools for financial professionals, but Model ML says it runs on open-source models and that doing so allows it to operate at about 2% of the cost of frontier models while delivering faster results.

Barnsley facing January nightmare as two departures predicted

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Barnsley are staring down the barrel of a first fourth-tier campaign since the 1978/79 season, and things may be made even worse in January, with EFL content creator and Reds fan Callum Fowler predicting that both Reyes Cleary and Patrick Kelly could be sold.

Daniel Stendel's side were unable to capitalise on some momentum and 12 goals in a couple of cup wins in the week leading up to their trip to fellow bottom-four side Wigan Athletic on Saturday.

A 2-1 defeat at the Brick Community Stadium saw the Tics climb out of relegation danger, leaving the Tykes winless in nine league outings, picking up just two points in that time. They're only off the bottom thanks to a superior goal difference compared to Bromley, who have the same record as them after 13 games.

It's looking pretty bleak at Oakwell right now, and the prospect of losing their best players in January is looking even more likely at this moment.

Despite Barnsley's dire situation, there's no doubt that they currently employ two of the brighter young stars in the division. Both Cleary and Kelly have a ceiling that surpasses the third tier, and perhaps could see them playing top-flight football in their careers.

Cleary burst onto the scene last season with 20 goal involvements in 30 league starts, and while he hasn't been as influential so far this year, his quality is still there, as seen with the cross he delivered for the Reds' equaliser against Wigan on Saturday.

Arguably even more concerning is the potential loss of Kelly, though. Only Derensili Sanches Fernandes and Oliver Norwood have created more open-play chances than the Northern Irish international in League One this season, and he's been the main source of everything good at the club.

Do you think both Reyes Cleary and Patrick Kelly will be sold by Barnsley in January? Let us know in the comments.

Cleary's star power from last season is almost leaving Kelly to operate without the eyes on him, but speaking on the Pyramid podcast, Barnsley fan Callum Fowler and co-host Tom Garratt discussed the possibility of losing them both.

"I just don't see how it turns," Garratt said. "I'll be amazed if you [Barnsley] keep Cleary and Kelly," to which Fowler responded, "We won't; they'll both go, 100%. Cleary will at least; he'll go on New Year's Day.

"Can you imagine that team without Cleary and Kelly? It's basically that team at Wigan with no Cleary, so you don't get the goal, because no one can put that ball in."

If there's one thing that the current regime at Barnsley under chairman Neerav Parekh can attest to doing well, it's ensuring that the club squeezes as much as they can out of buyers when they approach the Tykes for their players.

The likes of Liam Kitching, Mads Andersen, Michal Helik and Carlton Morris have all sold for seven-figure fees since the club came down to League One in 2022, and while undisclosed, it's likely that the late winter sale of Davis Keillo-Dunn to Wrexham eclipsed that, too. Some suspected above £2 million.

If anything, though, that ability to sell well just stokes the frustration surrounding the current hierarchy, as not a lot of that money, if any of it, is reinvested into the playing squad. Barnsley continue to take chances on young players, and it's led to this steady decline down the League One table.

Fowler doesn't think that, if Cleary and/or Kelly are sold this January, they will be replaced adequately, and then a Barnsley squad which is already in trouble will be even worse.

"It's heart over head at times, because I'm thinking, 'surely they'll chuck some money at this in January,' but they never have, not once," he continued.

"They've put the tickets as they have for the Wednesday game [£35 for adults], almost like one last cash grab; they'll sell Cleary and Kelly and bring in nothing."

It's close to universally agreed that Daniel Stendel has Barnsley coached well — at least, better than their league position would suggest — but the general naivety of the squad and the youthful nature often leads them to fade away in games.

The Tykes have led in seven of their 13 games so far this season, but have won just two of those. They've conceded 11 times in the final half hour of games, and four times in the final 10 minutes.

FLW gets AI to rank the friendliest clubs in the second tier this season

The majority of the Barnsley fanbase are still backing Stendel and know that it isn't his, nor the players', fault that they're in this position. A protest is being planned online ahead of their game against Sheffield Wednesday on Sky Sports this Friday, and time will tell what impact that could have.

Plainly put, though, if Barnsley don't strengthen in January, they may be set for the drop. If they lose two of the better creative players in the league, there may be no way back at all.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Photographer Captures Eerie Images of Looming Data Centers in Virginia

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Photographer Captures Eerie Images of Looming Data Centers in Virginia

A photographer has set about documenting data centers in northern Virginia — an area that has the highest concentration of data centers in the world.

Stephen Voss’s unnerving photographs show giant box buildings looming over housing developments, baseball fields, and cemeteries.

“Three years ago, I was driving back from a photo shoot in Virginia when I noticed a data center for the first time, in all its drab, boxy glory. Here was the physical reality of something we’ve somehow agreed to call ‘the cloud’,” Voss tells PetaPixel.

The disconnect, Voss says, stuck with him. Over the next three years, he made over 100 trips to data centers in northern Virginia, exploring how these facilities intersect with the communities and places where people live their lives.

“Where does a data center end and someone’s neighborhood begin? What does it look like when the infrastructure behind our digital lives becomes the view from someone’s backyard?” Voss asks.

The project became Every Past and Tomorrow, a book Voss intends to self-publish via Kickstarter.

“Most of us rarely think about the infrastructure behind our online lives — where our photographs are stored, our music is streamed, or an AI prompt gets answered,” Voss says.

“For people living near data centers, those buildings are part of their everyday lives and I don’t think we should discount what it means to live right near one.

“In some neighborhoods, the sounds of cooling fans and natural gas turbines can be heard inside of their homes. People watch their kids play baseball at fields with 75-foot data center walls just past the outfield fence.”

During the course of his project, Voss says he’s witnessed forests cut down, empty fields become enormous buildings, and places where he once made photographs closed off behind fences.

“Every time I return, I see a landscape changing and felt this urgency to document as much of it as possible,” he adds.

Voss says the landscape is changing so much that Google Maps can’t keep up.

“Street View and satellite imagery often show an empty field where there’s already a building,” he says. “So, I spend hours driving around, searching through online permits and county records, and returning to places again and again to see how they’ve changed over time.”

Over the course of the project, Voss gained a drone license so he could take to the air to better show the relationships between data centers and the neighborhood.

However, bringing massive nondescript buildings to life is no easy task, and he started to see the giant blank walls as a canvas.

“I dug through AI research papers, terms of service, and old science fiction,” Voss explains. “At night, I projected words from those texts onto the data centers themselves — returning the language of this technology to the structures that make it possible.”

Voss says that more than once security guards came and spoke to him as he projected text onto the buildings, making him hard to miss.

To support Voss’s book Every Past and Tomorrow: The Physical Landscape of AI, head to his Kickstarter page. More of his work can be found on his Instagram and website.

Can you use a keyboard and mouse with an Xbox Series X?

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There's no question the PS5 has handed the Xbox Series X and S their collective rear ends from a sales perspective this console generation. That's a shame for Microsoft fans. Even though there are drawbacks you should understand before buying an Xbox Series X, for my money, it's still an excellent console. It's also one that supports a rich suite of accessibility options, as does its little sibling. One of the biggest? Fairly robust keyboard and mouse support.

Using traditional PC peripherals on a console might seem a bit outfield, but hey, the Dreamcast was doing it a quarter of a century ago, so there's definite precedent. There are actually a ton of Xbox games that offer at least partial mouse and keyboard support. Let's get into the mouse and keyboard options that should work with the Xbox Series X/S, and also discuss how to set them up. Plus, we'll examine just how many games support non-gamepad control schemes on Microsoft's current-gen consoles.

You sure can... but there are caveats. While Microsoft has done a commendable job at providing accessibility features for Xbox fans, mouse and keyboard support on Xbox Series X/S isn't universal across all games. Additionally, of the titles that do work with PC-based peripherals, not all of them offer native support. That means certain actions in non-native titles will require you to have a controller nearby to progress.

There are more restrictions, too. There's currently no way to connect a Bluetooth mouse or keyboard to either Xbox Series console. For those looking to hook up their favorite PC gizmos, you're looking at either wired peripherals or ones that come with 2.4GHz dongles.

That's slightly annoying. The best Xbox Series X/S accessories are generally wireless because you're likely sitting a decent distance away from your TV while playing your favorite games. Unless you keep your Xbox at a desk like a PC, wired mice and keyboards are probably out of the question for everyday living room play. Dongles it is, then.

Oh, and there's one more catch. You can't use a mouse to navigate around the Xbox dashboard, which is a bit of a bummer.

Setting up a keyboard and mouse on an Xbox Series X/S is relatively simple, but functionality beyond that is going to vary based on the specific game. The easiest and most practical way to get started with PC peripherals on your Xbox is to plug in a wireless keyboard dongle and a mouse dongle: You can use the two USB-A slots on the back of either console, or the one at the front.

From there, you can utilize the keyboard for typing on the Xbox dashboard (though as previously mentioned, mouse navigation is a no-go). Once you launch a game, things are a tad up in the air. The title may recognize your PC-style peripherals immediately, and you can start pointing and clicking. Or, you may well need your trusty Xbox controller to navigate into the settings menu, where you should be able to select mouse and keyboard inputs, if the title supports them. Microsoft provides the platform, but accessibility options are up to the individual developer, so this workflow will vary game to game.

Basically, prepare to always have your gamepad in reach if you want to use typical PC peripherals with an Xbox Series console. Just in case.

A surprising amount. We're not quite talking PC levels here, but the list of Xbox games with current mouse and keyboard support (as reported by Pure Xbox) shouldn't be sniffed at by WASD fans. Well over 100 titles feature levels of mouse and keyboard functionality at present, though native support isn't guaranteed in all of them. That means you'll occasionally have to break out your Xbox controller to navigate menus. If you hop on the Xbox Store, a game's listing page should tell you what level of mouse and keyboard support a title offers. Those that don't natively offer traditional PC controls will be marked as "Limited."

Looking for a few big hitters that support native mouse and keyboard controls on Xbox Series X/S? You'll find Microsoft Flight Simulator 2024, Halo Infinite and Gears of War: E-Day. Considering one of those games demands complex simulation controls that never quite felt at home on a pad, and the other two involve landing headshots on alien beasties from afar, mouse and keyboard sounds like a great way to experience all three. I always thought Master Chief's 2021 open world was overly criticized, and now I'm eager to replay it on Xbox, sniping hordes of Brutes with my favorite mouse, the Inphic F9 Mecha.

There are also competitive shooters like Overwatch that support cross-platform play, and will automatically pair you with fellow mouse and keyboard users if the game detects a PC-style control scheme on console. After all, in a fast-paced online shooter, bringing a gamepad to a mouse-and-keyboard match is like wielding a pillow in a lightsaber battle.

There are a number of benefits to using desktop peripherals with an Xbox Series X/S. Some aren't all that sexy — oooh, I can type my custom license plate in Forza Horizon 6 a teeny bit quicker — but others have far more practical use cases.

For simulation or strategy games with multiple mechanical layers and rich inventory systems, mouse and keyboard inputs are simply superior. I've had great experiences with these devices on my Xbox Series X playing Jurassic World Evolution 3, even though the dino-park management title does work pretty well with a controller. Keyboard support also gives you more quick buttons to assign should an Xbox title support it, which can definitely be useful in games that involve heavy inventory management, like Cyberpunk 2077.

The main reason you want keys and a mouse, though? For fans of first-person shooters, gamepads can never come close to the accuracy of blasting bullets with the utter precision of a mouse. Halo Infinite, Overwatch, the Call of Duty series and S.T.A.L.K.E.R. 2: Heart of Chornobyl are all well-situated for mouse and keyboard play, among many other headshot-heavy games.

MFA, MA, and PhD Programs to Apply for by Early 2027

A list of arts-related graduate programs to explore and apply to before deadlines close.

These MFA, MA, and PhD programs are accepting applications now, with deadlines between December 2026 and February 2027. Many of the programs below are fully funded, with tuition waivers, stipends, and teaching assistantships.

The MFA in Visual Arts + Sound Art attracts emerging and mid-career artists from the United States and around the world to join a vibrant creative and intellectual community. Students are taught by internationally celebrated artists with the highest concentration of museums, galleries, nonprofit art spaces, independent collectives, and artist-run organizations.Deadline: January 20, 2027 | arts.columbia.edu

As Canada's #1 university for art and design, ECU’s two-year MFA and MDes programs integrate creative exploration, research and critical inquiry, connecting you with faculty, industry and community partners to work on projects with real-world impact. The dedicated studio facilities, interdisciplinary research opportunities, and a collaborative graduate community allow students to push the boundaries of their practice to bring their ambitious ideas to life.Deadline: January 25, 2027 | ecuad.ca

IDSVA offers a research-based, low-residency PhD in Visual Arts: Philosophy, Aesthetics, and Art Theory designed for artists, curators, writers, and creative thinkers. Through close mentorship with distinguished faculty and a globally engaged curriculum, students explore, question, and challenge how we understand art, culture, and human experience. Beasley (BBGI) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Record digital margins as Beasley trims debt and expands its sports push.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Beasley Broadcast (BBGI -1.90%) prioritized the expansion of its digital operating margins and the reduction of its debt obligations during the third quarter. Management reported that while the overall advertising environment remained challenging, particularly within national and local agency channels, structural cost reductions implemented over the past two years have begun to fundamentally improve the economics of the business. The company successfully reduced its first lien debt to $3.6 million through asset sales and equity financing while achieving record profitability in its digital segment. The company stated that it is evolving its sales organization from a traditional radio company into an omnichannel marketing platform with a focused emphasis on sports media and local direct relationships.

Operator: Hello, everyone, and welcome. I will now turn the call over to Ilana Goldstein.

Ilana Goldstein: Good afternoon, happy Friday, and welcome to Beasley Media Group's third quarter 2026 earnings call. Today's conference call and webcast will contain forward-looking statements about our future performance and results of operations. These statements involve risks and uncertainties described in the risk factors section of our most recent annual report on Form 10-K, as supplemented by subsequent filings with the SEC. Today's webcast will also include certain non-GAAP financial measures within the meaning of Item 10 of Regulation S-K. Reconciliations to the most directly comparable GAAP financial measures are available in this morning's news announcement and on the company's website. A replay of today's call will be available for five days on the company's website at www.bbgi.com.

Today's press release is also available in the site's investors and press room sections. I will now turn the call over to Beasley Media Group Chief Executive Officer, Caroline Beasley.

Caroline Beasley: Good afternoon, everyone, and thank you for joining us. Over the past several quarters, we've discussed our three priorities guiding our strategy. Number one, stabilizing and rebuilding core revenue. Number two, scaling a higher margin and more controllable digital business. And three, strengthening our balance sheet. In the third quarter, we continued to advance these priorities. However, the advertising environment remains challenging, but we are seeing the financial benefits of actions taken over the past two years. Let me begin with our operating performance. Third quarter net revenue was approximately $42.8 million, compared with $47.6 million on a same-station basis. Adjusted EBITDA was approximately $1.3 million, compared with approximately $3 million on a same-station basis.

While profitability remains below prior year levels, our results reflect the significant work underway to fundamentally improve the economics of our business. Over the past two years, we've taken deliberate steps to reduce our operating cost structure, streamline our organization, and prioritize revenue streams that generate stronger, more sustainable margins. On a same-station basis, revenue declined approximately $4.8 million year-over-year, while operating expenses were down $3.7 million, limiting the decline in station operating income to approximately $1.1 million. These savings reflect the structural changes implemented over the past two years, including our most recent actions in May. Importantly, not all revenue carries the same value to our business, and our strategy increasingly reflects that distinction.

Within digital, we have deliberately shifted our focus away from lower-margin third-party products and toward higher-margin O&O offerings, where we have greater control over our inventory, pricing, customer relationships, and profitability. That shift has contributed to lower digital revenue in the near term, but the improvement in profitability demonstrates why we believe it is the right approach. Despite a $1.4 million decline in same-station digital revenue, digital operating income increased nearly $300,000 year-over-year, reaching $3.1 million, and our digital operating margin expanded from approximately 26% to a record 32%.

We are generating more digital operating profit on a smaller revenue base, and that is an important distinction and reflects the progress we are making towards building a higher-margin, more scalable business. At the same time, we continue investing in the content, technology, and commercial capabilities that support our O&O platforms, strengthen our local advertiser relationships, and position us for sustainable, profitable growth. Our brands remain deeply connected to their communities, and our content attracts audiences across broadcast and digital platforms. Our focus is on converting that engagement into higher-quality revenue. Rebuilding revenue remains our focus. While substantial work lies ahead, the third quarter shows that our cost initiatives are producing tangible results.

Turning to the balance sheet, we made further progress on deleveraging. On May 1st, as everyone knows, we completed a comprehensive debt restructuring. Since then, we pursued additional transactions to strengthen our financial position and give us greater flexibility to execute our operating plan. In September, we completed a $5 million registered direct offering and private placement led by A.G.P., Alliance Global Partners. We recognize that raising equity capital is an important decision, particularly as we focus on long-term shareholder value. We evaluated this transaction against our strategic priorities and determined that it would better position Beasley for the next phase of our transformation.

In September and October, we completed the sale of two radio stations to EMF, along with two parcels of real estate to two separate parties. Together, these asset sales generated more than $10 million in net proceeds, which we directed towards strengthening the balance sheet. As a result of these actions, outstanding first lien debt has been reduced to approximately $3.6 million. Turning to our revenue outlook, the advertising environment remains challenging, particularly in traditional national and local agency channels, as Ilana Goldstein will provide more color. Political also developed differently across our markets versus what we initially expected. Detroit performed particularly well, with political exceeding $2 million.

Spending in several other markets, including Las Vegas and Florida, fell below our expectations. We booked over $4.5 million in political advertising across the full election cycle, with $1.3 million of that booked in third quarter. Although below our initial expectations, political was a meaningful source of incremental revenue in the second half of the year. Kevin will provide more detail on market-level political performance and the opportunities remaining in the next election cycle. Stepping back, the third quarter reinforced several important points. Our cost structure is more efficient, our balance sheet is stronger, and our efforts to build a more profitable digital business are producing measurable results.

These results are particularly meaningful given our continued portfolio optimization and deliberate shift away from lower-margin third-party digital products toward higher-margin O&O offerings. We are demonstrating that we can generate greater profitability from a more focused digital business, even in a challenging revenue environment. Looking ahead, our priorities remain clear: rebuilding core revenue, accelerating growth in local direct and digital, and continuing to improve the profitability and efficiency of our operations. With that, I'll turn the call over to Kevin to discuss our operating performance, political, and digital strategy. Kevin?

Kevin LeGrett: Thank you, Caroline, and good afternoon, everyone. Caroline walked you through the work on our cost structure and our balance sheet. One of the biggest areas of my job is the other half of that equation, which is revenue. I'll start with where we are. As Caroline stated, total revenue was down $4.8 million on a same-station basis. We're not happy with that. But inside that number, this business is changing and changing quickly, and it's changing in the right direction, exactly where we're steering it. Three facts from the quarter. First, local direct revenue grew 7% on a same-station basis. That is the business where we own the relationship with the advertiser, with no one between.

Local direct is the revenue we can control, and we controlled it. Second, as Caroline stated, our digital operating margin reached 32%. That is a record and the first time we've been above 30% in this company's history. Digital revenue may have been down in the quarter, and Ilana will take you through that, but our owned and operated products are now 65% of our digital revenue. We are selling more of what we own and less of what we resell. Fewer digital dollars in this quarter, but more profitable ones. That's a key indicator for us. Thirdly, our audience. Our stations reach nearly 17 million consumers every week.

Our company-wide rating share grew 3% in the quarter, and we hold a top three station in seven of our markets. The audience is not the problem. Our job is to monetize the audience we already have. The pressure that we feel was in the channels we do not control day-to-day, national network and local agency business. That decline is not unique to Beasley, and we are not planning for it to reverse, which is exactly why the model must change. We are evolving our sales organization from being a radio company to a local omnichannel marketing platform, one that combines the trust and scale of broadcast radio with the precision of streaming, podcasting, social, live events, and community engagement.

A local business owner does not wake up wanting to buy radio first thing. They want customers, and they want their cash registers to ring. Our mission is to help brands and businesses reach consumers at every touchpoint in the local journey, in the car, on the phone, in the feed, and at the event, and then to show that advertiser what we did for their business. That part really matters. We are deploying attribution and measurement across the platform now. Because brand growth earns the first order and accountable results earn the renewal, our investment here is really making a difference. We get the trust and scale from broadcast, the precision from digital, and proof from measurement.

That is a platform that we have and we are continuing to add to. The clearest example of that platform today is sports. In Boston, 98.5 The Sports Hub is the model for that. It is the home of the New England Patriots, the Boston Bruins, the Boston Celtics. It is not a radio station that just carries games. It is broadcast, streaming, podcasts, social, and live events, built around teams a city cares about deeply and sold to advertisers as part of a relationship. We have the same foundation in Philadelphia with the Philadelphia Flyers and the Philadelphia 76ers, along with The Fanatic, and in Detroit with the University of Michigan Athletics.

During third quarter, we added to that roster. Beasley is now the official year-round audio partner of the Vegas Golden Knights. Under a multi-year agreement, we have the English language and the Spanish language flagship. We signed this partnership less than three weeks before the NHL season, and advertiser response has already exceeded our expectations for the window we had. We are not sharing figures today, but what I will tell you is that it gives our Las Vegas team something every advertiser in the city wants to stand next to and be part of. Sports is appointment listening in an on-demand world. Fans show up live, they show up loyal, and advertisers follow them.

Based on what we have built in Boston and what we are seeing already in Las Vegas, we intend to keep building and expanding our sports portfolio. A platform only matters if we can sell it, and we are also making changes with how we sell and how we execute in all of our markets. The first is structure. We are moving to one selling organization with audio and digital in a single funnel, every seller carrying the same full portfolio, and market leaders whose job is defined around revenue generation. One team, one funnel, one number. The second is technology.

In September, we announced a multi-year agreement with Futuri and completed the deployment of their TopLine Enterprise to our sellers. TopLine combines AI with our own sales and audience data, as well as outside market intelligence. It supports prospecting, account research, meeting preparation, proposals, and campaign reporting. We did not buy a tool. We rebuilt the sales process around one. Our sellers review and approve everything it produces. AI does the research. Our sellers own the relationship. It is early, and I will report back on adoption and results when we have more data. The third part is talent. For decades, the way this industry found sellers was to hire them from a competitor across the street.

That is expensive, and it does not add one new seller to the business. We are building our own farm team. Our sales development representatives, our SDRs, are early career hires with one job, prospecting. They find and qualify new local business and hand those leads over to account executives in our markets. The best of them earn an account executive seat down the road. During the quarter, we brought on another group, and members of our first group have already moved into account executive roles and are closing business and participating in their local markets. We have stopped waiting for the talent market to send us sellers, and we are developing them internally. Turning to political.

As Caroline talked about, our full 2026 election cycle revenue, we booked $4.2 million in political advertising, $1.3 million of that falling in Q3. That is below where we expected it to be. But here's what happened. Political dollars follow the map. Early in the year, through the primaries, the map lined up well with our portfolio. In the general election, outside of Michigan, it really didn't. National dollars were concentrated in a small number of Senate and congressional races, and national committees and outside groups spent their money elsewhere, primarily in TV. The races moved, and the money moved with them. Michigan is the other side of that story. Where the map and our portfolio lined up, we won.

Michigan had a competitive Senate race and an open race for governor, and our Detroit stations generated more than $2 million, roughly half of what our company total was. When the money came to Michigan, it came to us, and our shares demonstrate that. Boston, Augusta, Charlotte also contributed meaningful dollars. I will add one more point because it matters. In North Carolina and Georgia, the Senate races were on our map and in our projections, but we did not capture the share of dollars we should have, as those races were not as competitive, and those dollars were reallocated in other parts of the country.

90% of our political revenue came through our national channels, whether it was campaigns, political action committees, or outside groups. I can tell you that after leading political with multiple audio companies through many cycles, it's very clear, political is a windfall, not a foundation for your business. We will compete for every dollar of it year in and year out, but we will not build the company's plan exclusively around it. What do we see next? We see the possibility for an earlier than we've ever seen presidential cycle, beginning possibly middle of 2027, with many on the Democratic side and the Republican side vying for nominations. That will be good for business and good for the industry.

What's next for Beasley? There's three more pieces of this rebuild that we are underway. We're cross-market selling, so an advertiser can now be served in every one of our markets and throughout the nation through the seller they already trust in their local markets. In addition, we're in the midst of our company-wide push on first quarter, generating revenue and driving real meaningful dollars to all of our markets. We're moving into podcasting, and we're focused more on selling audiences than selling shows through some really tangible partnerships that we've created. Each is early, and I will bring you the results as they develop. Let me close with this.

The cost work and expense discipline has bought us some time. The revenue work is what we do with it and continue upon the growth that we've seen here in third quarter in local direct business, as well as driving our digital dollars into a profitable arena. Local direct is growing, digital margin is at a record level, our audiences are holding up, and we have a platform in sports and beyond that advertisers want to be part of. Our work over the past year has established a stronger commercial foundation. The priority now is to turn it into a more consistent revenue growth and improved profitability.

With that, I will turn the call over to Ilana now to review our third quarter financial performance in depth. Ilana?

Ilana Goldstein: Thank you, Kevin. As Caroline mentioned, third quarter net revenue was approximately $42.8 million, compared with $51 million in the prior year quarter. Revenue declined approximately 16% on a reported basis and 10% on a same-station basis. Revenue continued to reflect pressure in agency advertising, partially offset by growth in local direct business. Local direct revenue increased 7% year-over-year on a same-station basis. We view high single-digit local direct growth as a milestone. In recent quarters, we have focused on reducing churn and increasing our share of existing customers' advertising budgets to stabilize revenue. This quarter's growth supports that strategy. National revenue, excluding political, declined roughly 18%, while local agency declined roughly 22%, both on a same-station basis.

These results reflect ongoing challenges in traditional agency-driven advertising, which we expect to continue declining through the remainder of 2026 and into 2027. Digital represented roughly 23% of total company revenue during the quarter, or 24% excluding political. Reported digital revenue was approximately $9.8 million, compared with roughly $13 million in the prior year quarter. On a same-station basis, digital revenue declined roughly 12% in the third quarter, while year-to-date revenue remains up roughly 4% compared with the prior year period. Advertising demand remained mixed during the quarter. Strength in several key verticals was offset by softness in more discretionary and consumer-facing categories. Gaming generated roughly $3 million in revenue, up 17% from the prior year period.

This remains an important category for Beasley, and its growth is encouraging amid broader pressure on traditional advertising. Automotive performance was mixed within the category, but the broader auto category experienced modest growth this quarter versus prior year period. Domestic auto dealer spending was relatively flat year-over-year, while import auto dealers grew roughly 13%. Although the broader environment remains challenging, these core dealer categories stabilized this quarter and are showing early signs of sustained improvement heading into 2027. Other areas of growth included insurance, banking, and food advertising. Our focus is on building on these areas of strength, rebuilding underperforming categories, and diversifying revenue through local direct relationships and digital capabilities.

Operating expenses, excluding corporate expenses, depreciation, and amortization, were approximately $39.1 million, compared with $46.1 million in the prior year period. This was a reported reduction of roughly $7 million, or 15%. The reductions reflect structural cost initiatives implemented over the last two years, including our comprehensive program executed in May. Because most May initiatives were implemented partway through the second quarter, that quarter captured only a partial benefit. The third quarter provides a more complete view of the ongoing savings. Reported station operating income, or SOI, before depreciation, amortization, and corporate expenses, was approximately $3.6 million, compared with $4.9 million in the third quarter of 2025.

On a same-station basis, SOI was approximately $3.7 million, compared with $4.8 million in the prior year period. We also incurred roughly $413,000 in severance expenses related to organizational restructuring. $113,000 represents corporate severance, while the remaining $300,000 of this pertains to operational headcount. Roughly $1 million in other expenses pertaining to the several transactions which have occurred in the last quarter have also been identified as non-recurring adjustments. Corporate expenses totaled roughly $3.9 million, compared with $2.2 million in the prior year period. This quarter included restructuring, financing, and strategic transaction costs. Excluding applicable non-recurring items, corporate expenses were roughly $2.7 million.

The comparison also reflects a $526,000 franchise tax benefit received in Q3 2025 that did not recur this year. Year-to-date, corporate expenses are down over $1 million, excluding severance and transaction fees. Adjusted EBITDA was approximately $1.3 million, compared with $3.9 million in the third quarter of 2025, and approximately $5.3 million in the second quarter of 2026. Year-over-year and sequential declines reflect continued revenue pressure, including the absence of contributions from the divested Fort Myers market and several other stations. Cost reductions helped mitigate the impact. We remain focused on converting structural savings into improved profitability as we stabilize and rebuild revenue. Depreciation and amortization expense was roughly $1.6 million, compared with $1.5 million in the prior year period.

Interest expense declined to roughly $1.3 million from $3.3 million in the third quarter of 2025, reflecting the May 1st debt restructuring and lower outstanding debt. Our new second lien notes include PIK interest, while the reduction in reported interest expense reflects the broader changes to our capital structure. Results also included a non-cash impairment charge of roughly $6.4 million related to FCC license, partially offset by roughly $2 million in gains on asset disposition. CapEx for the quarter was roughly $565,000. Now, turning to the balance sheet, we ended September with roughly $16.5 million of cash and cash equivalents. However, $11.4 million of this was used to pay down first lien debt in early October.

Total debt outstanding at September 30th was roughly $127.4 million, consisting of $15 million in first lien notes, $99.3 million in second lien notes, and $13.1 million drawn upon our ABL. However, following the mid-October paydown of first lien debt, our current capital structure is as follows: Total debt outstanding for mid-October was roughly $116.2 million, consisting of only $3.6 million in first lien notes. We have made significant progress on our capital structure. We began the year with a substantially higher debt burden, but restructuring, asset sale, and targeted financing transactions have meaningfully improved our financial position. We remain focused on reducing leverage, improving liquidity, and positioning Beasley to generate more sustainable free cash flow.

In 2027, we expect to continue benefiting from structural cost reductions while rebuilding revenue, improving digital profitability, and expanding commercial opportunities from our existing infrastructure. Our financial priorities remain consistent: improve EBITDA and free cash flow, continue deleveraging, and build a more sustainable revenue base. With that, I will turn the call back to Caroline.

Caroline Beasley: Okay. Thank you, Ilana, and thank you, Kevin, for your comments. As everyone mentioned earlier, the advertising environment remains challenging, and we are not satisfied with the pace of revenue recovery. Even so, progress on our cost structure, balance sheet, and strategic initiatives is evident. Looking ahead, our fourth quarter revenue is currently pacing up low to mid-single digits on a same-station basis, or slightly down, excluding political, of approximately $2.8 million in the fourth quarter. For the full-year 2026, we are currently pacing down in the mid-single digits on a same-station basis. We are encouraged by the improving revenue trends heading into year-end, supported largely by growth in our local direct and digital businesses.

Heading into 2027, our priorities remain unchanged. We are focused on stabilizing and rebuilding revenue, improving digital profitability, converting our lower cost structure into stronger EBITDA and free cash flow, and refinancing the company with a focus on divesting non-strategic assets in order to reduce existing debt. The transformation we began over two years ago has required difficult decisions and significant operating changes. Work remains, but we believe we are entering our next phase with a stronger financial foundation, a more efficient operating model, and growth opportunities. Finally, I would like to take a moment to thank our employees across the company for their continued commitment and hard work throughout this transformation.

We look forward to updating you on our progress during our next conference call. Thank you very much.

Operator: This concludes today's call. Thank you for attending. You may now disconnect.

Jito’s Lucas Bruder says crypto builders need to ‘slow down’ and fix security weaknesses

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Jito is expanding its security team and using AI for defense as Bruder calls the next two years critical for crypto builders.

Jito Labs co-founder and CEO Lucas Bruder says crypto developers need to pump the brakes on building to focus on security as attackers use AI tools to find weaknesses in their defenses.

Speaking to The Block on Tuesday at the Solana Breakpoint conference in London, Bruder said the next two years are going to be pivotal for teams to fix unresolved issues in their software, which he referred to as "technical debt."

"I’m a fan of moving fast, but now I think the next two years are gonna be super critical to slow down a little bit, fix your tech debt, go back and solve it," he said.

Bruder noted that Jito has been working on bolstering its security operation for the past year and a half. The company hired a security lead from Galaxy, added another security employee six months ago, and is now looking to hire two more.

"It's very intense out there," he said. "We've seen that the people that are doing this are very patient and very motivated. And so you just have to be very careful at a lot of layers of security."

For crypto companies in particular, this could mean improving how they manage multi-sig wallets, deploy software, and tighten hiring procedures.

"It's really a process that you need in your company," Bruder said. "So it’s not an afterthought, it’s like you have to bake it into the way that you work."

AI is also now being put to use on both sides of the security war. Asked about using AI to review code for vulnerabilities, Bruder said Jito has been doing similar work internally.

"On one hand, like they are using LLMs to attack you and on the other side, you can use the same LLM to help defend yourself," he said.

"You really just have to be proactive," Bruder added. "Don’t wait, just get ahead of it."

AirPods Pro 3 keep getting better, here’s what’s new in iOS 27

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

AirPods got a variety of new features this fall in iOS 27, several of which are exclusive to AirPods Pro 3 (now only $179). Here’s everything new.

Custom EQ is a feature that some AirPods users thought Apple would never ship, but iOS 27 adds it.

With Custom EQ, Apple now lets you set your own sound profile for AirPods. Per Apple:

AirPods are designed and engineered by Apple to faithfully represent music, TV shows and movies, and calls. If you prefer a different sound profile, you can customize how AirPods represent any audio played.

You can change the EQ by going to your AirPods’ settings ⇾ Audio & Routing ⇾ Equalizer ⇾ Custom. From here, you can adjust the sound profile for low, mid, and high frequencies.

Custom EQ is available on AirPods Pro 3, AirPods Pro 2, AirPods Max 2, AirPods 5, and AirPods 4.

Apple GymKit has been available for years, but only for Apple Watch. It lets you pair and sync workout data with compatible cardio equipment, like treadmills, ellipticals, indoor bikes, and more.

In iOS 27, GymKit expands to support iPhone using AirPods Pro 3’s heart rate sensor. My colleague Zac gave it a try:

The setup is simple. I tapped my iPhone to the treadmill, picked Indoor Walk (Indoor Run is also offered), and started the workout from the treadmill. With GymKit, workout data is stored privately on iPhone and removed from the equipment.

Once the workout started, the treadmill received heart-rate data from AirPods Pro 3. Meanwhile, the Fitness app on iPhone received the treadmill’s distance, pace, incline, and calorie data. Treadmills know details that an iPhone and AirPods can’t know on their own, especially incline and precise belt distance.

For more details on iOS 27’s GymKit feature for AirPods Pro 3, check out Zac’s full hands-on below.

Read more: iOS 27 basically turns your iPhone and AirPods into an Apple Watch at the gym

For most AirPods users, the single biggest upgrade in iOS 27 is Siri AI.

Siri AI is available on compatible iPhone hardware in iOS 27, and Apple made sure to expand full support for the AI assistant to AirPods too.

AirPods are essentially becoming an AI wearable thanks to the new Siri. It will let users do a lot more than the current Siri. Here are some key features via Apple’s website:

Earlier this year when AirTag 2 launched, Apple brought Precision Finding support to Apple Watch for the first time. Until now though, it’s been limited to AirTag 2.

But with iOS 27 and watchOS 27, Apple expands Precision Finding on the Watch to AirPods Pro 3.

AirPods Pro 3’s case includes the latest second-generation Ultra Wideband (UWB) chip, letting you find it with greater precision and step-by-step guidance right from your wrist.

With every batch of new AirPods features that debuts, the AirPods settings screen in the Settings app gets more cluttered.

But in iOS 27, Apple has a welcome fix: it’s redesigned and reorganized all AirPods settings.

Now, you’ll find AirPods settings organized into more categories and submenus, accompanied by colorful icons. It makes finding the right setting way easier and more intuitive than before.

Axe Compute (AGPU) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Customer prepayments, not debt, funded its first profitable quarter.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Axe Compute (AGPU -5.74%) completed its transition to a pure-play AI infrastructure provider during the third quarter after selling its legacy oncology business. Management reported the company's first profitable quarter on an operating and net income basis, driven by sequential revenue growth and gains on digital assets. The company stated that its business model utilizes customer prepayments to fund the acquisition of dedicated GPU clusters, which avoids the need for corporate debt to finance growth. Management indicated that it has secured a multiyear contract book expected to generate over $700 million in annualized revenue once global AI factories reach full deployment.

Erin McMahon: Good morning, and welcome to Axe Compute's Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Call. I'm Erin McMahon, Chief Marketing Officer and Head of Investor Relations at Axe Compute. Joining me today are Christopher Miglino, Chair and Chief Executive Officer; Jeremy Yaukey-Witter, Chief Financial Officer; and Kyle Okamoto, President. Today's call is audio and slides. A replay and the presentation will be available on our Investor Relations site after the call. Before we begin, today's remarks include forward-looking statements as referenced on Slide 2. Important disclosures. Before we begin, a reminder that today's remarks include forward-looking statements. These include statements about signed contract value, customer prepayments, deployment timing, annualized run rate, margins and financing.

They involve risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially. Please see the risk factors in our most recent Form 10-K and Form 10-Q, our other filings with the SEC and the full statements on this slide. We undertake no obligation to update these statements, except as required by law. We will also refer to operating metrics such as total contract value and annualized run rate, and we'll refer to certain non-GAAP measures, including adjusted EBITDA. Definitions and reconciliations are in this presentation and in our earnings release. With that, I'll turn it over to Chris.

Christopher Miglino: Good morning, everybody, and thank you, Erin, and thanks for joining the call this morning. I'm Chris Miglino, the CEO of Axe Compute. We told you last quarter, we might do this call from our deployment in Sweden. Well, there's not enough snow there yet. So you get us from nice warm Miami. The installation is going great there in Sweden, and we look forward to being there in the future. We had a great quarter, and we accomplished a lot. I'll start with the numbers that matter the most this quarter. Revenue was $7.2 million, up 125% from $3.2 million in the second quarter.

We did that while posting our first quarter of positive operating income of $6.8 million and our first quarter of positive net income of $11.5 million. We ended the quarter with $147.7 million in cash. This is our first quarter posting results purely as a GPU-as-a-Service business following the sale of our legacy medical business in September. That revenue growth was funded by our growing customer base, responsible spending in growing our current business and divestment of previous obligations from the legacy business. We did not add corporate debt related to the incremental revenue growth. Jeremy will take you through the details later in the call.

The headline is simple: revenue more than doubled, the quarter was profitable, and we're just getting started. For anyone new to the story, here is what we do. Our core business is to build, own and operate dedicated AI infrastructure. We co-engineer each cluster with the customer. We own the hardware and our team runs it around the clock. We partner with data center owners to rent their power and space. Every build that we do sits on a multiyear take-or-pay contract with an enterprise-grade customer and the customer prepays. Our builds are in the United States and Sweden, and we source capacity worldwide. Our team talks to data center developers all over the world.

We deploy the latest NVIDIA GPUs, working with partners, including NVIDIA, Supermicro and Penguin Solutions and work with those data centers that we seek out globally. Many customer relationships start with our product called Axe Access. It's a shared capacity offering, and they tend to grow from there into a dedicated build. You can think of this part of our business as a lead generation and client development product. We have clients that start out as small compute users that have grown much larger over time. I want to review how we finance growth. This has been a major conversation in our industry recently and is different for every neocloud business. It explains the numbers Jeremy will show you.

Every build is its own project. It starts with a signed contract, multiyear and take-or-pay. The customer prepays at signing and the prepayment funds a significant portion of the hardware. Where a project needs financing, that financing sits inside that project entity and is secured by the project's own contract and hardware. Axe owns the cluster and operates it for life of the contract. Then we do it again for the next build. What that means in practice, each build is contracted and prepayment is deposited before we order the hardware, each project is designed to stand on its own, and our growth is paced by signed contracts and customer cash.

We've talked about the large contracts we've signed within 8 months of business, and these market statistics illustrate the market demand that we're seeing. AI infrastructure spending is forecasted to reach $1.5 trillion this year and $2 trillion in 2027. Global data center capacity is expected to roughly double to 200 gigawatts by 2030. And token usage, which is a reasonable proxy for real workloads, grew 25x in 10 months. Usage is growing faster than infrastructure can be built. That is the shortage that we sell into, and we sell into it with capacity that is contracted and a substantial prepayment of typically 20% to 40% is received before we build. Market demand is strong and capacity is coming online.

Our unique value to the market of choice, experience, global reach and trusted partnership resonates with our customers, and we intend to continue to capture that demand. Three major business updates since our last call aside from the amazing growth that we just shared. The team worked diligently to execute on all of these. So I'm very proud of these accomplishments. First, we sold our Helomics business, which is the AI cancer diagnosis business that was part of the public company when we started the AI infrastructure business. Axe Compute is now a pure-play neocloud. This translates into less operating expenses for the company. Second, our Georgia cluster contract.

The nature of the Georgia contract has changed significantly since we signed it, and it has changed for the better. First, as of September 30, we own the cluster, 2,304 NVIDIA B300 GPUs, energized and provisioned. Second, we paid off the cluster's asset-backed loan with cash on hand with no new equity issued. And we owe a deferred purchase price secured by the cluster and the customer extended the contract to 5 years through 2031. Owning the cluster means the full contract economics accrue to Axe. We expect approximately $352.6 million of revenue over the life of the contract with a significant improvement in gross margin and free cash flow as a result of this ownership.

This was a major milestone for Axe Compute, and it's the type of build we want to do going forward. I want to thank everybody that was involved in completing this transaction as it was a significant undertaking. Finally, I want to call out the contracts signed in Q3. We previously announced build contracts we signed in July that span $1.3 billion across the U.S. and Europe as well as $1.5 billion 5-year dedicated Blackwell cluster. Since our Q2 call, we've added an additional $266 million in contracts and expansion. That takes signed contracts value over the past 7 months to $3 billion. I'm repeating the July numbers for a reason.

Q3 revenue was $7.2 million, and none of the more than $3 billion from our build project is in that number yet. The revenue starts as each build goes live, and Kyle is going to show you where we are with every one of those. With that said, I'll turn the call over to Jeremy, and he can walk you through the details of the numbers.

Jeremy Yaukey-Witter: Thanks, Chris. I'll cover third quarter results and the balance sheet. All figures are as reported in our Form 10-Q filed on November 16, unless noted. I'm very excited to highlight our results for the third quarter of 2026, which are only possible, thanks to the great work our team is doing to serve customers. Revenue for the 3 months ended September 30 was $7.2 million compared with $3.2 million in the second quarter, an increase of 125% Revenue in Q3 was entirely contributed by Axe Compute Access. No revenue was recognized from Axe Compute Build in the third quarter. Operating income was $6.8 million compared with an operating loss of $16.4 million in the second quarter.

Operating income includes a $10.5 million gain on digital assets. Net income was $11.5 million compared with a net loss of $17.2 million. Net income also includes a $4.6 million unrealized gain on the equity securities and convertible note we received in the sale of Helomics. Adjusted EBITDA for the quarter ended September 30, 2026, was a loss of $2.3 million. We start from income from continuing operations of $11.7 million, remove $0.3 million of net interest income, add back $0.7 million of stock-based compensation, remove $9.9 million of unrealized gains on digital assets and remove $4.5 million unrealized gain on the DataMeds securities and convertible note. Those prepayments are recorded as contract liabilities and convert to revenue as we deliver service. Our largest asset was $366.7 million of compute infrastructure for our build clusters, including the Georgia cluster acquired on September 30. We also carried $92.6 million of prepaid compute and $38.1 million of leased right-of-use assets for third-party capacity. Cash and cash equivalents were $147.7 million. Contract liabilities tied to customer prepayments comprised the majority of our liabilities, both short term and long term.

During Q3, we also added a liability reflecting the deferred purchase price owed to Duos following the acquisition, $42.9 million face value carried at $36.6 million net of discount as of September 30, due by September 30, 2027, or earlier upon refinancing and secured by the cluster. NVIDIA describes an AI factory as infrastructure that runs an entire AI life cycle from data to training to high-volume inference where the product is intelligence itself. That's exactly what we hand our customers, not a pile of GPUs or a pile of infrastructure or hardware. It's a complete factory, GPU compute, CPU compute for orchestration and data preparation, high-speed networking, high-speed parallel file system storage, dedicated security and the software stack on top to orchestrate and ultimately generate tokens. This is a co-engineering effort, running their own production workflows against their performance criteria with their configurations before it moves into a steady-state service. So the takeaway: 4 AI factories, over 18,000 GPUs, and each one is really a complete AI factory delivered as a service and run by Axe Compute.

So how does a company our size deliver 4 AI factories at the same time? This slide is really kind of that layer cake answer. At the center, Axe owns what matters most: the design, the hardware, the customer relationship and day-to-day operations. Around that core, we've built a deep and elastic bench of partners and suppliers at every layer of the stack. Elastic is the word I want you to remember. This is not only about sheer capacity; it's about flexing up and down so we can run multiple builds in parallel without carrying a big fixed cost base ahead of demand.

For GPUs, systems and fabrics, we build on the latest and greatest AI infrastructure with suppliers like NVIDIA, AMD, Supermicro, Dell, Celestica and several others. Multiple system sources means we're never single threaded on one supplier's allocation or lead time, all while ensuring consistent design and architecture. For storage, WEKA and DDN keep those GPUs fed with data at full speed. For deployment and support, Penguin Solutions and [ computer center ] give us integration crews on demand, so we can rack, cable and burn in at more than one site at a time. Then there are sites and power, a critical component in any AI factory.

CBRE reported in September that North American data center vacancy hit a record low with about 80% of capacity under construction already pre-leased. That's why we work with a variety of partners and deploy multiple types of models from colocation to site ownership. So we've always have more than one path to space and power. And on financing, as Chris covered, we work with leading investment firms to put project-level capital behind each build. So multiple options at every layer. One partner's queue is full, we go to the next. That's how we build in parallel. Axe owns and operates each build and our partners flex with demand to increase scalability, which ultimately allows us to satisfy customers' demand.

Automation is tooling and telemetry that catches problems early and resolve many of them automatically, so our support capabilities can scale faster than our head count; and 24/7 support, a follow-the-sun NOC watching every cluster around the clock, Tier 1 to Tier 3 escalation and 1 accountable point of contact for every customer. But why does this matter so much? Because the AI factory-scale hardware fails every day. When Meta trained Llama 3 on over 16,000 GPUs, it logged 419 unexpected interruptions in 54 days, roughly 1 every 3 hours and more than half traced back to GPUs and their memory. The operators who win are the ones who detect, swap and recover fastest.

This is exactly what this team is built to do. And this talent is hard to find. In Uptime Institute's 2026 global survey, more than half of data center operators said they struggle to find qualified candidates. We are finding them and they are choosing us. Christopher Miglino: Thanks for joining us today. I appreciate the question. This is Chris Miglino. So we were just going through it yesterday. We have over 75 active data center opportunities to look at. There is a lot of capacity that is going to be coming online here in the next year or so. So the United States is going to control the distribution of the chips outside of the United States. And so that is going to push a lot more activity back into data centers in the United States. Benjamin Sommers: Got it. Super helpful. And then just kind of -- you made a comment on you're starting to see the shift more towards inference workloads. Just kind of how do you think about positioning your power portfolio for the longer-term shift in demand towards inference workloads?

Christopher Miglino: Kyle, do you want to take that one?

Kyle Okamoto: Sure. I think one of the main premises that we've built Axe Compute on is choice. And the reason why we've done that is because clients have a strong need to be in specific places to support things like inferencing. So rather than simply telling a client, this is where we're building out our next cluster, and if you want AI infrastructure, you're going to get this spec, this configuration and this location, we've come at it from we want to be able to say yes to where a customer wants to be. And the main reason for that, obviously, is inferencing, right? You need to be as local as possible while still thinking global.

And that's why we have an entire team dedicated to going around the world and finding the next places that we can put AI infrastructure for our clients. And I think that will only continue, right? If you look at the evolution of the Internet, the evolution of cloud, the evolution of edge computing, the evolution of IoT, right, all of these different waves of technologies, all started out very centralized and then ultimately got more and more distributed. And I think you'll see the same thing for AI infrastructure. So the ability to build a very fast and distributed set of AI infrastructure. Again, I talked about elasticity earlier, and we obviously talk a lot about choice.

I think those are 2 very important tenets going forward for the AI community.

Prediction: These Will Be Nvidia’s and AMD’s Stock Prices by the End of 2027 (Hint: Only 1 Is a Buy).

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

AMD's stock has a ton of growth priced into it already.

Nvidia (NVDA -1.06%) has had a rough few weeks with CEO and co-founder Jensen Huang resigning. That has cratered the stock, which is now down significantly from its all-time high. Nvidia's loss has been AMD's (AMD -2.41%) gain. The rival chipmaker’s stock has risen 16% since November began, while Nvidia's is down nearly 20%.

AMD investors couldn't have been more pleased. There's also the potential that some Nvidia investors are shifting funds into AMD shares to maintain their AI exposure without the mess of a CEO transition, which is a valid premise. However, I think most of the Nvidia saga will be wrapped up by the time 2027 ends, and the stock market will have returned to valuing both businesses for what they are, rather than based on how headlines are influencing them. So, what do I expect each stock to be trading at by the end of 2027? I think you'll be very surprised.

Leadership matters in a company. That's why the news of Huang’s rapid exit was so jarring to many investors. He has been the only CEO in its history, and he was instrumental in building the company into what it is today. Without him at the helm, some investors aren't as confident in Nvidia's prospects. Still, Huang didn't do everything at Nvidia. Countless others helped build Nvidia into what it is, many of its longtime leaders are still there, and one could take over next.

Nvidia's products remain superior to AMD's, and the company still has a wide moat thanks to the ubiquity of its CUDA software platform, which allows its GPUs to be programmed for whatever uses its customers have in mind. Because so much early AI work was done on CUDA, and because so many developers are comfortable in it, shifting out of the Nvidia ecosystem can be a chore.

While AMD's GPUs are growing more popular, they're only being deployed in certain applications. Meanwhile, Nvidia still holds a commanding market-share lead in data center GPUs and is still delivering faster sales growth.

NVDA Revenue (Quarterly YoY Growth) data by YCharts

Nvidia has recently delivered particularly impressive growth given how large its revenue base is relative to AMD's, but the market isn't giving the chipmaker credit for it.

With 2026 nearly wrapped up, it's OK for investors to start looking ahead to 2027.

Next year, Wall Street analysts expect Nvidia's revenue to grow at a 67% pace. While some may discount that growth rate given what's going on in the C-suite, I don't think that's a wise investment move. The reality is that Nvidia's largest clients already know what they're going to spend on chips next year, and they've let Nvidia know so these products are ready to go when they need them. During the Q2 earnings call, management gave guidance for 70% revenue growth in 2027. It's rare to see a company issue guidance for the upcoming year so far in advance. The only reason why Nvidia would do that is if it had high visibility into revenue, and nothing that has happened over the past few weeks will likely change its outlook for 2027.

According to the analyst community, AMD's growth rate is expected to exceed Nvidia's. Analysts project AMD will grow its revenue at an 84% clip. That would be a strong outperformance if AMD can pull it off, but considering its much smaller sales base, it's not growing nearly fast enough to make a big dent in Nvidia's lead.

Regardless of what transpires next year, the reality is that AMD's expected growth has already been priced into its stock, while Nvidia's hasn't.

NVDA PE Ratio (Forward 1y) data by YCharts

AMD is over three times more expensive than Nvidia based on next year's earnings projections. That's a huge price mismatch, and I think it's a bit absurd that AMD trades at that much of a premium when their growth rates are fairly similar. While I agree that AMD deserves a premium right now because its CEO is still in place, Nvidia has technological advantages and a massive market-share lead that cannot be discounted. Given how expensive AMD stock is, I forecast that its stock will be flat over the next year and will still trade at around just under $600 per share at the end of 2027.

Meanwhile, I wouldn't be surprised to see Nvidia's price-to-earnings ratio double to 20, which would also imply a stock price double that of today's level, about $330 per share.
