Ethereum Devs Weigh a Scaling Upgrade That Could Break 15,000 Apps

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ethereum Devs Weigh a Scaling Upgrade That Could Break 15,000 Apps

Ethereum’s upcoming Glamsterdam upgrade threatens to break as many as 15,000 smart contracts, developers warned during a core developer call Monday, forcing a debate over how much disruption the network should tolerate in the name of scaling.

The concern centers on two proposed changes, EIP-8037 and EIP-8038, designed to let Ethereum scale from 60 million gas to as much as 200 million gas per block, a roughly 3.3x jump that developers say would sharply lower costs for most users while keeping state growth in check.

The problem, flagged late in the development process, is that the changes break certain invariants, assumptions that some smart contracts were built to rely on.

“Multiple contracts were relying on invariants in the protocol that are modified by 8037 and 8038,” said Marius van der Wijden, an Ethereum Foundation developer who worked on the scaling effort.

He acknowledged that the team “wanted to do significant public outreach” but that timelines slipped amid reorganizations and budget cuts at the foundation. He said he shares “a lot of blame” as the last remaining scaling lead still at the EF.

Two security firms, ChainSecurity and Dedaub, presented findings from AI-assisted audits, with the combined search turning up roughly 200 distinct classes of affected contracts, most tied to gas-cost increases rather than the new mechanics themselves.

Van der Wijden put the highest estimate at 10,000 to 15,000 contracts, a “fringe subset” of Ethereum’s roughly 90 million, with impacted value he loosely pegged in the low hundreds of thousands of dollars among contracts not yet committed to upgrading.

App developers pushed back on the framing. Jango, who builds the Juicebox protocol, warned the change undermines “composability,” a core Ethereum value prop, and shakes “developer confidence in building immutable protocols.” A Uniswap developer urged that EIP-8037 “should definitely be reconsidered.”

The Ethereum Foundation already delayed Glamsterdam’s testnet fork over these gas-related security flaws. Developers stressed Monday that no decision has been made, with the question headed to Thursday’s execution-layer call.

But several signaled a strong desire to ship the fork as planned, arguing that removing the changes could delay Glamsterdam, and the broader roadmap toward post-quantum security, by four to six months, time they say Ethereum can’t spare as quantum and AI threats loom.

researchers turn simulated moon dust into 3D-printed bricks for future lunar homes

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

a 3D printing method fuses simulated moon dust into bricks without adhesives, opening possibilities for construction with lunar materials.

Researchers at the University of Arkansas have developed a way to 3D print interlocking building blocks from simulated moon dust, using heat to fuse the material together without glue or cement. Led by mechanical engineering professor Wan Shou and researcher Cole McCallum, the experiment explores how future lunar settlements could be built using materials gathered directly from the Moon’s surface.

The idea could reduce the amount of construction material that needs to make the journey from Earth. Instead of transporting cement and other building supplies into space, astronauts could eventually manufacture structural components using the mineral powder already covering the lunar landscape.

The team has produced a series of small, LEGO-like modules that can be assembled into larger structures and rearranged as needed. Each block is made entirely from a material that mimics lunar regolith, the loose layer of crushed rock and mineral dust covering the Moon.

Lunar regolith might resemble ordinary sand or soil, but its physical properties are quite different. Over billions of years, meteorites and other space debris have struck the Moon’s surface, breaking down rock into increasingly fine particles. Without wind or flowing water to gradually wear away their edges, these fragments remain sharp and angular, with an abrasive texture that NASA has compared to tiny pieces of glass.

The dust also behaves differently under lunar conditions. Its particles can become electrically charged and cling to nearby surfaces, making them particularly troublesome for astronauts and sensitive equipment.

During the Apollo missions, lunar dust damaged spacesuit materials and clogged mechanical components. Engineers planning future missions are still working out how to keep the powder from entering machinery, where its jagged edges can wear down moving parts.

For their experiments, the Arkansas researchers used Lunar Highlands Regolith Simulant, known as LHS-1, a specialized powder developed by the University of Central Florida’s Exolith Lab and supplied by Space Resource Technologies.

The material is made from terrestrial minerals selected to reproduce the composition and granular texture of lunar highland soil, drawing on samples collected during the Apollo missions.

Creating convincing artificial moon dust has become a field of research in its own right. Specialized laboratories and companies manufacture different regolith simulants to help engineers test lunar excavation systems and study the effects of abrasive particles on spacecraft hardware. These mixtures reproduce selected properties of lunar soil, although the real material has characteristics that remain difficult to replicate on Earth.

To manufacture the blocks, the team turned to a 3D printing method called laser powder bed fusion. A thin layer of simulated regolith is spread across a platform, where a focused laser traces the intended shape and heats the mineral particles until they fuse together.

The platform then lowers, another layer of powder is added, and the process repeats until a solid component takes shape. The approach allows the researchers to work with the regolith alone, avoiding the polymer binders or cement mixtures used in some earlier experiments with lunar construction materials.

Instead, thermal energy bonds the grains directly to one another through a process known as sintering. ‘There’s nothing else it’s binding to. The regolith powder is binding to itself,‘ explains McCallum, who was an undergraduate at the university when he began the research. For future construction on the Moon, this could eliminate the need to transport large quantities of adhesives from Earth.

The researchers also considered how individual printed components might come together to form larger structures. Drawing on the principles of traditional mortise-and-tenon joinery, they developed two complementary brick geometries: one shaped like a cross and the other resembling the letter H.

The cross-shaped piece acts as a projecting connector, fitting into the recessed profile of the H-shaped module. Together, the pieces can interlock without additional fastening materials. While the researchers describe the system as LEGO-like, the comparison refers to its modular assembly instead of the familiar rectangular bricks with circular studs.

The team has demonstrated small wall configurations and shielding units assembled from its cross-shaped and H-shaped pieces. Printing individual components could also offer more flexibility than manufacturing an entire enclosure with a large-scale 3D printer. Blocks could be produced in batches and replaced individually, allowing structures to be rearranged as the needs of a lunar settlement change.

The researchers are also investigating the possibility of using concentrated sunlight to harden lunar regolith. In their published study, they consider how optical devices such as Fresnel lenses or parabolic mirrors could focus solar radiation onto the powder, producing temperatures high enough to fuse the mineral particles together.

Earlier solar-sintering experiments have already demonstrated the potential of concentrated light to harden simulated lunar soil, although the Arkansas team’s own prototypes were manufactured using a laser. The idea could eventually allow construction equipment to draw on two resources already present on the Moon: regolith and sunlight.

The current bricks remain experimental, with porous surfaces and connections that require further refinement. Their strength also needs improvement before they can support substantial structural loads. ‘These structures we’re making can be self-supported,’ says McCallum, while acknowledging that the technique still needs development for load-bearing construction.

The idea has already been explored in another lunar construction experiment. In 2023, the European Space Agency (ESA) tested a method for creating roads and landing pads from simulated moon dust, using a powerful laser to melt the powder into a solid, glasslike surface.

The project, called PAVER, aimed to prevent passing rovers and landing spacecraft from kicking up abrasive lunar dust that could clog machinery and damage equipment. Researchers even produced interlocking, triangular paving elements, and proposed using sunlight concentrated through a large Fresnel lens to replace the laser on the Moon.

Further experiments under lunar vacuum and gravity conditions will be essential before the team can begin translating its laboratory experiments into a practical construction system. For now, the printed blocks are only a few centimeters across, and the researchers are continuing to investigate how their mechanical properties can be improved.

Still, the research offers an early glimpse of how future lunar settlements might manufacture their own building materials. With mineral powder gathered from the surrounding landscape and heat supplied by lasers or potentially sunlight, the basic components of a building could eventually be produced directly on the Moon, one layer of regolith at a time.

project: Additive Manufacturing of Lunar Regolith for Reconfigurable Building Blocks toward Lunar Habitation research institution: University of Arkansas researchers: Cole McCallum, Youwen Liang, Nahid Tushar, Ben Xu, Bo Zhao, Hao Zeng, Wan Shou collaborating institutions: University of Houston; Tampere University material: LHS-1 (Lunar Highlands Regolith Simulant) simulant developer: University of Central Florida, Exolith Lab

These 13 Dipsgiving Recipes Bring A Festive Twist To Any Holiday Potluck

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This year, we’re grateful for chips and dip.

There’s a classic Thanksgiving and a more laid-back Friendsgiving, but this year, we’re taking things a step further by hosting Dipsgiving. It’s exactly what it sounds like: Thanksgiving-inspired dips for dinner. For this spin on our favorite way to potluck, we gathered our best festive dips, from cranberry-laden starters to meaty mains and even a few sweet scoops to end the meal. So what are you waiting for? Grab your chips, your toasty bread, and any other dippers you prefer, and get to dipping!

A holiday cheeseboard is great and all, but why go to all the trouble when you can make this sweet, salty, creamy, and tart dip with a fraction of the effort? Fresh feta cheese is combined with herbs, garlic, and cream cheese, whipped until super-smooth, then topped with tart homemade cranberry sauce, toasted pistachios, and honey.

Corn dip is the creamy, cheesy party dip we just can’t get enough of. A cream cheese base with a hint of heat makes this corn-packed appetizer a crowd-pleaser at any gathering. While some corn dip is served cold, we prefer to serve mine hot.

Let's be honest: there's no party dip like classic spinach & artichoke. Don't get us wrong, it's perfectly creamy and cheesy on its own, but it's also ripe for additions and creative takes. One of our favorite ways to upgrade this party appetizer is to add everyone's favorite ingredient: bacon.

Don’t worry, we didn’t forget the pumpkin pie! Here, pumpkin pie spice, vanilla pudding, and fresh pumpkin puree are whipped into a light and fluffy dip that’s not as heavy as the classic fall pie, yet still has all of the festive flavors you love.

Not only is this cranberry brie dip perfect for holiday parties, it's the best way to use up those homemade cranberry sauce leftovers. This is one of our most-loved Thanksgiving appetizer recipes: not only is it completely delicious, but it couldn't be easier to make. You only need a handful of ingredients and under 30 minutes.

Whipped Ricotta With Olives & Roasted Red Peppers

Whipped ricotta is beyond easy to make, even though it sounds so delicate and fancy. That’s what makes this a perfect appetizer to serve for an intimate dinner party or the holidays. Topping with marinated olives and roasted red peppers gives the dip a briney flavor, and the red and green makes it extra festive.

This dip starts off with a base of cream cheese and mayo. The addition of Parmigiano, Dijon mustard, freshly ground black pepper, and a wee bit of anchovy brings the Caesar notes fully forward. I love to serve this dip with toasted crostini or pieces of romaine lettuce to simulate salad vibes, but there’s no shame in however you choose to dip.

We’ll use a dirty martini as inspiration for just about anything at this point. Take this dip, for example: we combined a tried-and-true dip base of crumbled feta and fluffy whipped cream cheese with iconic martini ingredients, like chopped green olives, pimento peppers, and a little bit of booze, and our newest obsession was born.

All this holiday app takes is a few minutes in your food processor to aerate your brie and turn it into something smooth, spreadable, and somehow even creamier. It’s super versatile too—you can mix it up with different jams, and serve it with an assortment of different crackers.

What do you do when you’re craving tiramisu, but you don’t have the time or patience to chill it for hours? Enter tiramisu dip. It’s got all the right flavors—creamy mascarpone, rich espresso, and a generous dusting of cocoa powder—and it comes together in just 10 minutes.

A party without dip is hardly a party at all, and onion dip tends to reign supreme at celebrations year-round. It’s creamy, savory, and perfectly scoop-able, so it’s no wonder it’s always the first to disappear. With this recipe, we take the classic onion dip one step further by incorporating deeply caramelized onions into the mix.

If you love deviled eggs but hate the hassle of filling them, then we have your new favorite party appetizer: deviled egg dip. It has all of the tangy, punchy flavors you love from the classic party app—think Dijon mustard, tangy dill pickle relish, and a touch of spice—but in a scoopable form.

Of all the incredible dips we’ve created over the years, our cranberry whipped feta dip is an internet and personal favorite—so much so that we just had to come back with an equally luxurious variation: this cranberry-topped whipped cream cheese spread.

Claude AI Predicts LIT Rebound Following Lighter Crypto Hack

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Forget the temporary dip tied to yesterday’s exploit. By New Year’s Day 2027, Anthropic’s Claude AI predicts Lighter crypto will deliver one of the sharpest post-incident rebounds the perp DEX sector has seen this cycle, the kind of violent recovery that turns a scary headline into pure rocket fuel.

The LIT price took a hit on yesterday’s exploit news, dropping more than 20% to $2.50. However, the team’s response and USDC fund recovery have seen the price recover to just under $3, surging +10% over the past 24 hours.

On November 16, 2026, Lighter, the high-performance decentralized perpetuals exchange that settles on Ethereum with zero-knowledge proofs, activated Guardian mode and paused all withdrawals and new pool creation.

On-chain data showed roughly $10.39M in USDC leaving the platform’s Ethereum settlement contract in a single withdrawal. Some researchers put the total impact closer to $15.5M across two large market-maker accounts. The attacker used a leaked API key tied to a trader’s account. The root cause was insecure custom signing code that reused cryptographic nonces, allowing the attacker to reconstruct the private key from public signatures.

With the key, the attacker created a public pool from the compromised account, acquired most of its shares, moved the victim’s USDC into the pool, and redeemed it. Importantly, Lighter’s core protocol and smart contracts were never compromised; this was an account-level key compromise, not a smart-contract exploit. Funds were rapidly swapped into ETH, with portions routed to Hyperliquid. LIT initially dropped by 20% on the news before buyers stepped in.

BREAKING: Hackers steal nearly $15.5 MILLION from Lighter using leaked API keys.$LIT crashed 18% on the news. pic.twitter.com/Rf2PsTSSRd

Lighter moved fast. Within hours, the team publicly confirmed the cause, named the attacker wallet, and made clear that the protocol itself remained intact. The Security Council recovered approximately $5M in USDC and returned it to the affected trader. Guardian mode was deactivated the same day and withdrawals resumed. New safeguards followed immediately: wallet signatures are now required for transfers into public pools, API key signatures are non-public, IP allowlists are planned, per-key permission limits are in place, and automated monitoring for nonce reuse is active.

Users running custom or third-party signing software were strongly advised to rotate keys and switch to Lighter’s official SDKs. The message was clear: the platform treated the incident as a user-key hygiene issue and hardened the surrounding controls without ever having a core vulnerability.

That combination of transparent communication, partial recovery, rapid resumption of operations, and concrete security upgrades is exactly the kind of response that historically flips sentiment from fear to “they handled it cleanly.”

LIT is currently trading in the $2.65–$3.00 zone after the sharp post-incident flush. The sell-off created a classic higher-volume capitulation low, with RSI on the daily timeframe dipping into oversold territory before printing a higher low.

Price is now reclaiming short-term moving averages while volume has expanded on the green candles, classic signs of aggressive dip-buying rather than distribution. On the higher timeframe, the broader structure remains constructive: a multi-month base that previously supported a run toward the $5+ region is still intact.

A sustained hold above the $2.80–$3.00 recovery zone would open measured-move targets that align with a full post-incident squeeze, especially if volume continues to confirm and the security narrative solidifies. The setup favors a sharp mean-reversion rally once the “protocol was never broken” story fully digests.

Lighter already competes in the high-performance perp space. Turning a high-profile but non-protocol incident into a demonstration of operational resilience and rapid hardening only strengthens its positioning. In a market that rewards clear recovery narratives, that edge can compound quickly.

The stage is set for exactly the kind of over-the-top, narrative-driven repricing that catches people off-guard when confidence snaps back.

While Claude AI predicts a monster Lighter crypto recovery between now and 2027, a presale play is stealing all the headlines. Bitcoin Hyper (HYPER) is less than two weeks from its public launch and has one singular focus: bringing high-speed financial applications to the Bitcoin ecosystem.

Bitcoin Hyper aims to enhance Bitcoin’s capabilities by introducing faster transactions and expanded decentralized finance (DeFi) functionality through a Layer 2 network built on the Solana Virtual Machine (SVM).

This project aims to combine Bitcoin’s liquidity and established investor base with the speed and low transaction costs modern decentralized applications require.

The HYPER presale has reportedly raised over $33.2M. Additionally, the project offers a 35% annual percentage yield (APY) for staking, allowing token holders to earn rewards on their investments.

As the planned November 26 launch date approaches, Bitcoin Hyper’s presale is entering a crucial phase. The project’s public debut is attracting interest from those following the growth of Bitcoin’s Layer 2 ecosystem, especially as it prepares for availability on centralized exchanges.

Get Ahead of the Next Blue Chip Presale Play

Claude AI predicts the maximum bullish target for LIT by January 1, 2027, at $12. This would mean a 4x run from current prices, putting the recent exploit in the rearview mirror and setting Lighter crypto up for a strong 2027.

This remains pure speculation and entertainment, not a realistic base-case outlook. The catalyst would require extraordinary coordination across the Lighter crypto team and a continued strong response to yesterday’s hack.

Juiced Scrambler Mini launched as a cooler (and safer) e-bike option for teens and parents

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Finding an electric bike for a teenager can be surprisingly tricky. Parents generally want something safe, manageable, and from a reputable company. Teenagers, meanwhile, generally want something that doesn’t look like it was picked out by their parents.

The newly launched Juiced Scrambler Mini looks like it could do a pretty good job of threading that needle.

The new youth-sized e-bike takes the mini-motorcycle styling of Juiced’s larger Scrambler models and shrinks it down around 16-inch fat tires, creating a bike designed for riders between 4’0″ and 5’3″ (122 to 160 cm). But perhaps more importantly for parents, Juiced also built in a PIN-protected speed limiter known as Speed Lock that lets adults decide just how fast the bike can go.

The Scrambler Mini ships set to a 10 mph (16 km/h) Beginner Mode, but its maximum speed can be adjusted anywhere from 1-20 mph (32 km/h) and then locked behind a four-digit PIN. Juiced notes that riders aged 8-12 should not exceed 10 mph, while riders 13 and older can use the bike at speeds up to 20 mph, subject of course to local laws and regulations. In almost every state and jurisdiction though, the bike will fit Class 2 designation for street-legal riding and bike lane operation.

That setup seems like a pretty smart way to approach youth e-bikes. Instead of buying a slow bike that a teenager may quickly outgrow – or handing them a much faster machine before a parent thinks they’re ready – the same bike can essentially grow with the rider (with speed titrated by a parent or guardian).

And from the kid’s perspective, it certainly doesn’t look like a safety-first children’s bike.

The Scrambler Mini gets the same sort of moto-inspired styling that has defined Juiced’s Scrambler lineup, including a long bench seat, dual-crown-style fork, chunky 16 x 4-inch tires, and several appropriately loud color options including Blackberry, Fruit Punch, Blueberry, and Pink Lemonade.

There’s some decent hardware underneath that styling, too. The rear hub motor is rated at 500W nominal and 1,092W peak with 80 Nm of torque. A removable 48V 10Ah battery using LG cells provides 480Wh of capacity, with Juiced claiming up to 40 miles (64 km) of range. Four levels of cadence-based pedal assist are included, as is a right-side thumb throttle.

The bike also features Talon hydraulic disc brakes with large 180mm rotors front and rear, 50mm of front suspension travel, an integrated backlit LCD, a headlight, and a rear taillight with brake-light functionality. The single-speed bike weighs 59 lb (26.8 kg), has a 27.15-inch seat height, and is rated for up to 275 lb (125 kg) of payload.

Those parts are far above what we normally see on e-bikes targeted towards teens, with most teen-focused e-bikes sporting cheaper components like mechanical brakes and rigid forks.

The Scrambler Mini is listed at $1,099, though Juiced is currently offering a Black Friday sale that drops the price by another $150 to just $949. The company says preorders are expected to begin shipping the week of December 7.

That price also comes with something increasingly important in the crowded youth e-bike market: an actual company standing behind the bike. Juiced was founded in 2009, quickly becoming a leader in the market for powerful and stylish e-bikes. But after the brand’s financial troubles a few years ago, Lectric eBikes stepped in to take Juiced under its wing in 2025. The relaunched Juiced now offers a three-year manufacturer warranty and US-based support, plus the peace of mind that comes with backing from North America’s largest e-bike company, Lectric eBikes.

That last part may be just as appealing to parents as the speed limiter, though the 3-year warranty is definitely a deal sweetener, too.

There’s always going to be some controversy around the idea of e-bikes marketed towards teens, but I’m for it when done responsibly. Let’s face it: e-bikes are incredibly popular and teens are already going to be riding them, even if it means borrowing a friend’s e-bike because they don’t have their own. So if they’re going to be riding, the least we can do is put them on a safer, higher-quality bike so they learn the right skills in the right environment. And hey, it sure beats teens sitting alone in their rooms texting each other all afternoon.

When it comes to choosing an e-bike, there are already plenty of cheap mini e-bikes and electric dirt bike-style machines floating around online, and many are undoubtedly going to look tempting to teenagers. The Scrambler Mini seems to take a different approach, giving young riders the styling, fat tires, throttle, and mini-moto attitude they actually want, while giving parents control over how much of that performance they can access.

It’s still an e-bike capable of 20 mph, and that means parents need to make decisions about where, when, and how their kids ride it. And there’s even an argument to be made for removing the throttle and turning it into a Class 1 e-bike in the beginning, requiring a teen to pedal it to engage the motor. But I’d much rather see that conversation happening around a bike with hydraulic brakes, lights, a programmable speed limit, and a real company standing behind it than around some anonymous electric mini-bike bought from the depths of the internet.

And if the teenager thinks it looks cool enough to actually ride, that’s probably the point.

Suno Moves Into AI Filmmaking With New Product ‘Visuals’

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The AI company is expanding to video and image generation with its latest launch, describing its mission as a "creative entertainment" brand rather than a strictly musical one.

Suno is pushing past the world of AI-powered music creation and into the world of videos and images with its latest launch, Visuals. With the new tools, users can generate up to 15 seconds of video based on written prompts, music input or images. The product can be used to create music videos, lyric videos or cover art, among other use cases. In a company blog post announcing the launch, the company’s chief product officer, Jack Brody, notes that Suno is a “creative entertainment” company that goes “beyond music.”

“Suno began with a simple belief: everyone is creative. With the right tools, people can make something original and feel the joy of bringing an idea to life,” writes Brody. Last month, Suno launched Speech, its first push past music generation, which allows for users to prompt their way into generating poems, spoken audio and meditations.

“Video had always been the logical next step in our vision, but until recently video generation was slow and too expensive for anyone to create but enterprises,” says Brody. “A new generation of models changes that and opens video generation up to creative entertainment for everyone.”

According to representatives for Suno, Visuals is powered by H3 Move, a model derived from H3 from MiniMax, fine-tuned/optimized for how Suno believes its community will use the product. Unlike other AI visual generators, Suno’s is made to align itself with the structure and story of a user’s musical creations.

The launch of visuals also unlocks new monetization for Suno. Visual credits are treated differently than song credits, and free-tier users cannot generate more than a single five-second video before hitting their credit limit. Any further use requires topping up for more money.

The blog post about Visuals is careful to note the “additional safeguards” added “to protect artists from unauthorized use of their likenesses or works, on top of the ones we already have in place for music.”

These new safeguards include “new text and visual filters intended to block uploads and prompts that try to use or create visual representations of specific artists, public figures, copyrighted works and IP.” The company also scans videos before sharing them with users to ensure they do not violate community guidelines against a range of “harmful content.” All visuals will also be embedded with watermarks before they can be shared off the platform.

“We are pairing these protections with in-platform user education. The first time a Suno user tries to upload a video or image to use with Visuals, we remind them the tool is for original creation, and to confirm they have the rights to any file they are trying to use,” the blog post explains.

Some of the ramifications of abusing the tool can include removing the content, restricting the account or even terminating a Suno user.

News of Suno’s Visuals launch comes about six months after OpenAI discontinued its short-form video generator, Sora, which could also generate music in the background as a byproduct of the service. At the time, copyright owners complained about the platform’s use of protected characters, brands and images. Billboard also found that the platform could, in rare cases, generate clips of copyrighted songs, like Pop Smoke‘s “What You Know Bout Love.”

In recent months, legal action against Suno has mounted. On top of an additional lawsuit from Universal Music Group and Sony Music surrounding alleged copyright infringement, Elvis Presley‘s estate and a class action group, including Jason Isbell, have also recently sued the company over alleged violations of their name, image, likeness and voice.

Suno has argued in lawsuits that its current models do not allow users to include the names of specific artists in prompts, nor produce outputs that mimic specific real voices. The complaint from Elvis’s estate argues that those claims are “unequivocally false.” They included links to videos capturing the use of Suno to play songs that sound remarkably similar to The King.

To read Suno’s full blog post about Visuals, click here.

Evercore: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Cost discipline is widening margins faster than revenue grows, a rare dynamic that could fuel shareholder returns.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Oct. 28, 2026, Evercore (EVR -0.07%) reported record adjusted net revenues, a non-GAAP metric that excludes standard accounting items, of nearly $1.2 billion. Long-term investors should care because advisory market share gains and expanding non-merger advisory businesses drive earnings higher even before private equity dealmaking fully recovers.

Senior managing directors (SMDs), the partner-level dealmakers who generate client fees, drive advisory revenue for the investment bank. With 50 SMDs still ramping up to full productivity, the firm holds substantial latent earning power within its existing headcount.

Evercore built fee streams outside traditional mergers and acquisitions (M&A), which include restructuring, equity underwriting, and digital infrastructure financing. These complementary practices generate consistent cash flow when corporate dealmaking slows.

Management lowered the compensation ratio, the share of revenue paid to employees, to 63% for the quarter. Lower overhead spending allows a larger portion of revenue growth to reach the bottom line.

Record deal backlogs and high client engagement demonstrate that corporate advisory demand is building across global markets.

Deep sponsor relationships, an expanding middle-market footprint, and product capabilities in debt advisory create durable competitive advantages. Management is watching financial sponsor activity and compensation discipline as transaction volume accelerates.

Capital returns through share repurchases and dividends remain priority channels for surplus cash flow as productivity rises.

Il Como e il sogno ottavi di Champions: per renderlo realtà servirà un altro storico miracolo

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

La League Phase di Champions è arrivata al giro di boa e il cammino delle quattro italiane, fino a questo momento, ci ha regalato tantissime sorprese. Alzi la mano chi avrebbe mai pronosticato, al momento del sorteggio, che il Como sarebbe stata la squadra di Serie A più in alto di tutte in classifica. Più in alto dell'Inter, più in alto del Napoli e più in alto della Roma. E invece è andata proprio così: la squadra di Fabregas, grazie alle vittorie contro Lipsia, Manchester United e Lens, con il clamoroso 1-9 del 4 novembre scorso rifilato ai francesi, si trova infatti al quarto posto, dietro soltanto a PSG, Real Madrid e Arsenal.

Il cammino dei lariani è stato notevole, lo abbiamo detto, ma niente è ancora deciso e le ultime quattro partite della League Phase saranno decisive, in un senso o nell'altro. Facciamo però l'inevitabile distinzione tra la qualificazione diretta agli ottavi di finale, che spetterà alle prime otto classificate al termine dell'ottava e ultima giornata, e la qualificazione ai playoff, ovvero il turno intermedio, una sorta di sedicesimo di finale, che vedrà impegnate le squadre che si piazzeranno dal nono al ventiquattresimo posto.

Quasi impossibile che il Como venga eliminato al termine della League Phase. I 9 punti raccolti finora potrebbero addirittura già essere sufficienti e le gare contro AEK e Betis in calendario prima della sosta di più di un mese rappresentano due occasioni per chiudere definitivamente i conti. Più difficile, invece, arrivare tra le prime otto, visto che a gennaio ci saranno le sfide quasi impossibili contro Paris Saint-Germain prima e Barcellona poi. Realisticamente la squadra di Fabregas ha quasi la certezza di un posto ai playoff, dunque, ma sognare non costa niente e visto l'attuale quarto posto, pensare agli ottavi di finale non è certo utopia.

THE AI TRUST DIVIDE: NEW SURVEY REVEALS HOW CONSUMERS FEEL ABOUT AI-GENERATED ADVERTISING

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Learn how to spot AI-generated images, avoid deceptive ads and how to use AI to your advantage while shopping online

BELLEVUE, Wash., Nov. 17, 2026 /PRNewswire/ --

BACKGROUND:Have you ever felt duped by an AI advertisement? You're not alone.

Experience the full interactive Multichannel News Release here: https://www.multivu.com/auros/9419751-en-new-survey-consumers-ai-generated-advertising

According to a new study commissioned by Auros (fka UserTesting) conducted by Talker Research, 43% of Americans have felt duped by an AI ad. The poll of 2,000 U.S. adults also found that 44% frequently see products advertised using some form of AI. More than half said they do not trust products advertised with AI to match their descriptions.

Consumers aren't just distrustful of AI; many also have trouble identifying what's real and what isn't. When shown real products, nearly half (52%) correctly identified them, but 31% thought what they saw was AI, and 18% thought they saw an AI-enhanced product. For retailers, that uncertainty can significantly affect how consumers perceive brands. According to the study, 36% of consumers believe a product is lower quality if AI is used in its marketing, while only 17% believe it's higher quality.

While consumers don't like when AI is used to sell to them, they view AI more positively as a shopping tool, with more than half (53%) saying AI has been helpful while shopping. Study data shows AI may play a bigger role this holiday shopping season too. 54% plan to use AI for some part of their holiday shopping and 37% believe AI will make it easier to find gifts.

In this segment, digital commerce expert Bobby Meixner reveals the biggest "tells" in AI advertising and how to avoid them. He also shares results from the study, how to differentiate between real, fake and AI enhanced images, and how to make AI work for you while shopping.

For more information, please visit: https://auros.ai/

MORE ABOUT BOBBY MEIXNER:Bobby Meixner is Vice President of Industry Strategy at Auros and an expert on technology, consumer behavior, and digital commerce. He has held leadership roles at eBay, Magento Commerce, Oracle, and Auros, with a career focused on how technology reshapes e-commerce, customer experience, and the relationship between brands and consumers. He is based in Los Angeles.

3 ways DDoS attacks knock websites offline, from clogged links to busy servers

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Volumetric, protocol and application layer attacks each exhaust a different part of a site. Here is what fails first and why.

A website can go offline without anyone breaking into it. No data is stolen and no password is cracked. The site simply receives more traffic than it can handle, and real visitors cannot get through. Attackers no longer need much skill or equipment to do this. Help Net Security reports that DDoS-for-hire platforms and hijacked internet-connected gadgets, known as IoT devices, make large attacks easier to launch. The danger to a site therefore depends less on who the attacker is than on how much traffic that person sends and which part of the site's connection it is aimed at.

Defending a site is mostly a problem of recognition, not strength. Any operator can block traffic. The hard part is blocking only the hostile traffic, because a flood of requests from an attacker can look just like a rush of real customers after a sale or a news story. An operator who shuts out everyone during an attack ends up with the same outage the attacker wanted, only by the operator's own hand. That is why a crude fix rarely helps. The defender has to keep serving the people who belong on the site while turning away the people who do not, and that has to happen while the attack is still under way.

Cloudflare, which sells protection against these attacks, sorted the main ways attackers use DDoS attacks against different parts of a connection, from the work of generating web pages to the bandwidth that links a site to the wider Internet and the firewalls between them. "The key concern in mitigating a DDoS attack is differentiating between attack traffic and normal traffic," Cloudflare said. That concern applies to every kind of attack, but the failure itself differs from one kind to the next. In one case, "the site went down" means a link too full to carry anything. In another, it means equipment too busy keeping track of connections. In a third, it means a server too occupied answering requests to serve real visitors. Knowing which failure is under way shapes what a defense has to tell apart and how quickly it has to act.

The crudest way to take a site offline never touches the site itself. In a volumetric attack, attackers fill the link that connects a target to the rest of the Internet until it has no capacity left, Cloudflare explains, and requests from real visitors never get through. The attack becomes a contest of raw size. A server can work perfectly and still be unreachable when the attacker sends more traffic than its connection can carry.

Few attackers control enough traffic to win that contest, so they borrow it. Some send requests from botnets, which are networks of hijacked machines that send traffic from many places at once and can't be shut off at a single source. Others use amplification. Floods and amplification attacks that abuse DNS, the Internet's address-lookup system, made up 34.3% of network-layer attacks, according to Help Net Security. Attacks built on DNS are therefore a large part of what defenders face at the network layer. Either method leaves a connection with no room for legitimate traffic.

The typical flood is small and short, and the giant attacks are rare. "Despite the hyper-volumetric growth, the median DDoS attack Cloudflare mitigated in the first half of 2026 remained short and small with 96.62% of network-layer attacks remaining under 500 Mbps and 90.60% ending in under 10 minutes," Cloudflare said, as reported by Help Net Security. The rare cases are becoming more common, and attacks larger than 1 Tbps rose significantly over the same period. A site that handles routine attacks has passed the common test but not the hard one, and that record says little about whether it could withstand an attack large enough to use up all its bandwidth.

A short attack leaves people no time to react. Many attacks were over in less than a minute, according to Help Net Security. An attack that brief can end before any engineer has a chance to step in. A defense against floods has to run automatically and all the time, and it has to decide what to let through without waiting for a person.

Against the bluntest attack, how fast a defender judges traffic matters as much as how much bandwidth it has.

A protocol attack takes a site down by overworking the machines that sit between its Internet connection and its web pages. These attackers don't try to fill the link to the wider Internet, and they don't try to make the server build pages until it runs out of effort. Cloudflare reports that these attacks use weaknesses in network layers 3 and 4, and that they use up the capacity of a site's servers, its firewalls or both. Those layers are the networking machinery beneath the web pages themselves, where traffic gets handled before any visitor sees a page. What gives way, then, is the site's own equipment. The machines run out of room to work, even though nobody has broken into them and nothing has been stolen.

Some of the machines that attackers overload exist to guard and organize the very traffic that swamps them. "Protocol attacks cause a service disruption by over-consuming server resources and/or the resources of network equipment like firewalls and load balancers," Cloudflare said. A firewall meant to screen out hostile traffic, or a load balancer meant to spread visitors across servers, can become the bottleneck itself. That changes how a site operator should read an outage. When attackers exhaust the machines rather than the bandwidth, a site that goes dark isn't simply short of connection space. Buying a bigger connection on its own leaves the weak point in place, because the equipment behind that connection still can't keep up with the work it is handed.

For a site under this kind of attack, the useful question is not only how much traffic arrived but which machine ran out of capacity to handle it.

In an application layer attack, attackers go after the server's own effort to build web pages, not the connection to the Internet or the equipment in front of the server. Cloudflare reports that this attack targets web page generation, the work a server does to answer HTTP requests. Attackers keep those requests coming until the server has no effort left for the people who actually want to use the site. These are the same HTTP requests a visitor's browser sends to load a page, which makes the hostile traffic the same kind as the legitimate traffic. A site under this attack is not jammed. Its server is too busy doing its normal job for the wrong people.

Attackers here count on the cost of answering a request as much as on the amount of traffic. Building a page for a visitor takes work, and attackers aim at that work. "The goal is to overwhelm the target with requests, since a single HTTP request can be expensive for the target server to respond to," Cloudflare said. The danger in this attack depends on how much work each request forces the server to do, not only on how many requests arrive.

Defending against this attack means judging requests, not only limiting them. Rate limiting, a general defense against DDoS attacks, lets a server take only so many requests within a given period. That cap keeps the workload within what the server can handle, but it limits how many requests get through, not which ones. Another general defense is a web application firewall, which filters traffic by rules. That filtering is the step that tries to separate hostile requests from real ones.

Against an attack made of the same requests a real visitor sends, protecting a site comes down less to absorbing traffic than to recognizing it.

Craigslist founder Craig Newmark has given away $500M. He hopes the midterms were ‘the beginning of the end’ for big money in politics

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Craig Newmark, who founded Craigslist in 1995, has given away more than $500 million, with some of that money going toward protecting American elections—and pigeons. Now Newmark is hoping the 2026 midterms mark a turning point for big money in politics.

Asked what Elon Musk’s midterm spending says about the limits of big money, Newmark said Tuesday at the Fortune 500 Innovation Forum in Detroit that he wants to see it as “the beginning of the end of the influence of really big money.”

“That may or may not be realistic,” added Newmark, who signed the Giving Pledge late last year. “We won’t know for another 10 years or more.”

Musk spent roughly half a billion dollars on the midterms and had little to show for it, considering most of his donations went to Republicans. Historically, money usually wins. In 2020, the top-spending House candidate won about 88% of the time.

Newmark said he has stepped back from trying to influence Washington himself.

“I just decided that for myself, Washington politics is no country for old nerds,” he told Fortune Editorial Director Kristin Stoller, who moderated the panel. “My prior efforts have all failed, with the exception of where I chat with the people on the front lines of fighting off attacks from our foreign adversaries.” Even there, he said, “I’ve been told to keep my mouth shut.”

The foundation lists voter protection among its priorities, and election security and fighting disinformation have also been focuses of his giving. Just last week, he made a five-year, $28 million pledge to the Bob Woodruff Foundation.

Stoller opened the panel with a clip of billionaire Pershing Square CEO Bill Ackman telling Fortune Editor-in-Chief Alyson Shontell that philanthropy is “vastly less efficient at solving problems than capitalism,” from an interview published in August.

Asked whether Ackman had a point, Newmark said: “I do think he’s right at times.” But Newmark added that AI safety and cybersecurity have become “a kind of big national security thing,” and that “neither industry nor government is getting the job done.” He said he’s spending the next two days in Washington on the issue, “helping people much smarter than me.”

Cybersecurity has long been a priority for Newmark. In 2022, his foundation committed more than $50 million to a coalition of groups working to protect Americans from cyberattacks.

“I really am a nerd, and I’m not the right guy for this,” Newmark said. “But I’m building back-channel relationships with AI companies and others to get the work done. Like the Batman says, I’m not the nerd you want, but I’m the nerd you got.”

Meanwhile, Ford Foundation President Heather Gerken—whose organization was created in 1936 by Henry and Edsel Ford and funded with Ford company stock—argued philanthropy and capitalism aren’t at odds.

“Philanthropy is the product of capitalism, but it also is what makes capitalism possible,” Gerken said during the panel. She’s currently leading the biggest overhaul of the $18 billion foundation since 2015, and on Tuesday, said the Ford Foundation is making big bets on democracy and the rule of law, economic mobility, and innovation, while changing how it works to be more nimble and deliver “surge funding” where it’s needed.

But Gerken also said philanthropy shouldn’t take credit for the midterm results.

“Philanthropy should not be taking any credit for a win of one party or another,” she said. “If they did, they’re breaking the law.” But when people say the election went smoothly, “what they’re missing is the invisible work that made that possible,” she said, pointing to lawyers who fought challenges to state election systems.

As for what’s next, Newmark said he’ll keep working to protect the country from AI-based cyberattacks while supporting veterans and military families—“and seriously, pigeon rescue.”

Sydney Lake is an associate editor at Fortune, where she writes and edits news for the publication's global news desk.

Should I buy a gaming console just to play GTA 6? That depends on your FOMO.

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Grand Theft Auto VI is out extremely soon, if you hadn't heard. Are you prepared?

By that, we mean do you have a PlayStation 5 or Xbox Series console at the ready for Thursday's big launch? If not, you're going to miss out on the biggest gaming launch of this year and many other years. FOMO is a natural human reaction to these sorts of things, and here at Mashable, we fully understand the pain of not being sure whether or not you should buy a console just for GTA 6.

So, let's lay out the cases for and against doing that.

The primary and most obvious reason to buy a console for GTA 6 is that it's the only real way to play it at launch on Nov. 19. There is technically another way that we'll cover in a little while, but for all intents and purposes, you cannot have the same experience everyone else is having without buying a PS5 or Xbox Series console.

We don't know this yet for sure, but there's a possibility that GTA 6 is an incredible video game. You probably wouldn't want to miss out on that. What are you going to do while everyone else is having an amazing time parading through Leonida, read books? Come on, that sounds boring.

One thing that's also worth remembering is that other video games exist. A lot of them, in fact. If you buy a PS5 or Xbox Series console, you'll have access to the last six years of current-gen releases, some of which are extremely good. They might even be better than GTA 6! As a fun bonus, through the magic of backward compatibility, ports, remasters, and remakes, you'll also be able to play countless video games from previous generations of hardware, too.

Lastly, both consoles (at least the models with disc drives) work as 4K Blu-ray players, if you're into collecting physical versions of movies. That's cool, too.

Both consoles have been hard to find in stock (thanks in large part to GTA 6), but the Xbox Series S and PS5 Digital Edition are both in stock at Amazon, as of this writing.

First, foremost, in the biggest, boldest font you can imagine: You shouldn't buy a console just for GTA 6 because they're stupid expensive these days.

Both Xbox and PlayStation have recently increased the prices of their consoles due to real-world economic concerns. The cheapest model right now is a $499 Xbox Series S, and Rockstar hasn't shown us that version of the game yet, leading to speculation that the game is better on PS5, which starts at $599. If you want the best possible version at launch, you'll need a PS5 Pro, which costs $899 — and is currently out of stock at most retailers.

In other words, there just isn't a particularly affordable way to play GTA 6 traditionally at launch, unless you already own the hardware.

There are, of course, other reasons to not get a console for GTA 6. There exists a slim likelihood that the game just isn't that good, though again, we have no idea at the time of publication. It also won't have an online component at launch, and no one knows when it will get one. For a lot of people, GTA Online is the only GTA experience that matters, and that audience will have very little to do in the new game until some nebulous point in the future when Rockstar decides to cater to them.

Maybe you just don't care that much about non-GTA video games, either. If that's the case, it's hard to recommend spending this much money on a box that will only play one game.

There are two main ways to play GTA 6 without buying a PS5 or Xbox Series machine. One of them doesn't sound all that great, while the other one will require some patience.

To be clear, there are multiple massive reasons why you might not want to do this. For starters, it involves a monthly subscription. Beyond that, Microsoft recently imposed strict monthly hour limits on Cloud Gaming. With this system, you will be able to play at most 15 hours of GTA 6 per month. Many people are going to play more than that in the first weekend it's out. Even if those aren't concerns, game streaming is subject to the limits of your home internet, meaning that latency and buffering can and will occur. That version of the game will also be visually downgraded to some degree, per Rockstar.

If game streaming (understandably) doesn't work for you, the last option is to wait for the PC version. Unfortunately, Rockstar has not said anything about when GTA 6 will launch on PC, though a recent report pegged it as coming in early 2027. That will likely be the best version of the game in terms of visuals, but only if you have the requisite hardware. For those who already have capable gaming PCs, this might be the best option for playing GTA 6, provided you're patient enough to wait. For those who don't, this is going to be even more expensive than buying a console, so maybe don't bother.

Prima l'inizio da fenomeno, poi il nulla: Adzic è sparito. Così delude Sassuolo e Juventus

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Vasilije Adzic in estate ha lasciato la Juventus in prestito per 500mila euro, ripartendo dal Sassuolo, che nell'accordo ha inserito anche un diritto di riscatto fissato a 12 milioni di euro, con i bianconeri che si sono tutelati a sua volta, inserendo una contro-opzione a 13,7. Il montenegrino classe 2006 reclamava spazio dopo aver solo fatto intravedere il suo talento e così a Reggio Emilia con Alberto Aquilani ha trovato l'ambiente che sembrava essere ideale per lui.

Le prime 6 partite della stagione che ha disputato tra Coppa Italia e Serie A lo hanno visto segnare 4 gol e fornire 1 assist, punendo anche proprio la Juventus, sua ex e forse futura squadra di appartenenza. Il livello raggiunto gli aveva permesso anche di essere convocato in Nazionale, dove aveva dato un altro saggio delle sue qualità: 2 gol in 4 gare, che avevano portato così il totale a 6 centri e 1 assist in 10 match. Neanche nei sogni poteva immaginarsi di meglio.

Le aspettative su Adzic sono cresciute, la pressione è aumentate e, malgrado fosse chiaro a tutti che non potesse tenere questi rimi a lungo, i tifosi e non solo hanno iniziato a pretendere quel qualcosa in più che non è riuscito a dare. Dalla sfida dell'11 novembre contro il Milan a oggi, il centrocampista offensivo è sceso in campo 6 volte, di cui 4 con il Sassuolo e 2 con il Montenegro, non lasciando mai il segno. E così nella crisi neroverde, che ha portato all'esonero di Aquilani, lui è finito ai margini, partendo solo in 3 occasioni da titolare e non entrando nemmeno contro Frosinone e Lazio. Con D'Aversa pure lui dovrà mandare un segnale.

8 Biggest Snubs in the 2026 Game Awards Nominations

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Game Awards has announced its official list of nominees for this year’s prizes. Among the nominees are plenty of obvious contenders, such as Grand Theft Auto 6, Resident Evil Requiem, and The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time. There was stiff competition across several categories, meaning that some worthy games — and Polygon favorites — didn’t make the shortlist for certain prizes. These are the biggest TGA nomination snubs of 2026.

As one eagle-eyed Resetera user pointed out, Sony just broke a streak that dates all the way back to 2015. This marks the first time in 11 years that a first-party PlayStation release hasn't received a Game of the Year nomination. During that 11-year streak, Sony took home three GOTY trophies: God of War (2018), The Last of Us Part 2 (2020), and Astro Bot (2024). This year's big first-party game from PlayStation was Marvel's Wolverine, which is currently sitting at a 75 on Metacritic and incited a whole lot of debate about difficulty and UI design. The game was nominated in two categories: Best Performance for Liam McIntyre and Innovation in Accessibility.

This outcome won't come as a surprise to Polygon's Oli Welsh, who predicted Marvel's Wolverine missing out on a GOTY nomination and snapping Sony's impressive streak all the way back in September.

There were a ton of fantastic video game performances this year, and somebody’s favorite was bound to get left out. Fire Emblem: Fortune’s Weave fan favorite Jimmie Yamaguchi gave an outstanding performance as the oddball scholar Fabio, and by every measure, it deserved a nomination. In less capable hands, Fabio’s starry-eyed devotion to Fortune’s Weave co-protagonist Dietrich could have been played for laughs, but Yamaguchi makes the budding romance between these men believable and heartfelt.

Manni L. Perez was nominated for GTA 6, presumably for portraying Lucia Caminos. (Funnily enough, developer Rockstar Games has not yet officially confirmed Perez is actually playing Lucia, despite longstanding online rumors.) Apparently, the actor who played co-lead Jason Duval didn’t make enough of an impact with players to score a nomination.

Blizzard’s World of Warcraft has had a massive year, with the launch of its reimagined Classic experience, World of Warcraft: Forever. The team also released major updates for the modern version of the beloved MMORPG, like March’s Midnight expansion. Those changes weren’t enough to move the needle with voters, and WoW was also left out of the Best Community Support category.

The popular farming sim, which entered early access in 2024 before its full release this year, may have more than 26,000 “overwhelmingly positive” reviews on Steam, but that wasn’t enough for it to snag a nomination in any category. Best Debut Indie might have been Fields of Mistria’s best shot at a prize, but it wouldn’t have been out of place in Best Sim/Strategy or the broader Best Indie category, either.

Despite a wobbly third act, Fortune’s Weave is one of our favorite games of 2026, one of the best games available for the Nintendo Switch 2, and one of the best-reviewed games of the year. Surprisingly, developer Intelligent Systems’ epic strategy RPG only managed to pick up one nomination, for Best Role-Playing Game. It stands a decent chance of taking this one home: other nominees include Final Fantasy Resonance, Monster Hunter Stories 3, The Blood of Dawnwalker, and Trails in the Sky 2nd Chapter.

Pearl Abyss’s open-world adventure game was an early-year frontrunner for a GOTY nom, even after middling reviews and a rocky launch. While it wasn’t a critical darling, it did have a pretty massive online following that made the prospect of several nominations seem likely. Surprisingly, Crimson Desert will compete in just one category: Best Community Support.

Bungie’s extraction shooter managed to snag a nomination for Best Multiplayer Game, which it frankly stands little chance of winning against a field that includes two indie chart-toppers and a well-regarded Call of Duty. However, the stylish game was overlooked in the Best Art Direction category, perhaps partly due to the shadow cast by a 2025 plagiarism scandal where Bungie admitted to using an artist’s designs without proper credit. (Bungie later credited her as a Visual Design Consultant.) Marathon’s retro-future meets Y2K cyberpunk aesthetic truly stands out in a sea of Unreal Engine 5 homogeneity, and it’s a shame to see it overlooked here.

Both the Best Indie and Best Debut Indie categories have some stiff competition this year. However, no independent game managed to get a nomination for Game of the Year in 2026. While there weren’t a ton of indie contenders for the top category this year, Polygon’s Oli Welsh had the co-op adventure Big Walk pegged as a dark horse, though developer House House’s recent AI controversy likely didn’t help matters.

The lack of an indie GOTY nominee represents a pretty massive shift from the 2025 crop, which included a record-setting three games developed by independent studios: Hades 2, Hollow Knight: Silksong, and Clair Obscur: Expedition 33. Sandfall Interactive’s much-loved RPG managed to take home the top prize and a total of nine Game Awards, the most wins by a single game. 2024 also saw one indie crack the category, Balatro, though the top honors went to Astro Bot.

The Game Awards 2026 will stream live on Disney+, YouTube, and Twitch, on Thursday, Dec. 10, starting at 7:30 p.m. EST / 4:30 p.m. PST.

Will Grand Theft Auto 6 take home Game of the Year?

Jensen Huang says he would be ‘honored’ to return as Nvidia’s CEO after resigning

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang signaled interest Tuesday in returning to lead the computer chip company in his first public statement since he resigned last week following revelations of an extramarital affair with an alleged Chinese spy.

Some tech industry figures, including SpaceX and Tesla CEO Elon Musk, have called for the reinstatement of Huang, a high-profile booster for the artificial intelligence industry who oversaw Nvidia’s development as the key provider of chips driving advanced AI models. President Donald Trump, a Huang ally who has spoken with him frequently, has also endorsed the comeback bid.

“If the Board of Directors decides that my return is in the best interest of our shareholders, our employees, the company, and America, I would be honored to answer these calls and return as CEO,” Huang said in a social media post.

Huang acknowledged making “serious mistakes” in his personal life but said that he’s received an “outpouring” of support from employees, investors, business partners and customers for his return.

Nvidia did not immediately respond to a request for comment on Huang’s statement. On Monday, a person familiar with the board’s thinking who insisted on anonymity to discuss it told The Associated Press that the board was not currently considering Huang’s return.

The Santa Clara, California-based company was scheduled to release quarterly earnings later Tuesday. Nvidia Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress is expected to speak with Wall Street analysts on the earnings call for the first time since being named interim CEO.

A petition drive seeks Huang’s return

A “Stand with Jensen” petition set up last week has gathered momentum, gaining signatures from current and former Nvidia staff, investors and other Silicon Valley supporters. The petition calls on the board to bring back Huang while an independent investigation proceeds or until there’s “compelling evidence” that his departure is in the company’s best interests.

“We are concerned that Jensen was removed without meaningful due process,” the organizers said in an open letter. “We have not been shown evidence substantiating the allegation that the woman at the center of this controversy was a spy.”

Trump suggested Monday that the ex-CEO could return, saying Huang “learned a big lesson” and that he “would certainly not mind” if he was reinstated.

Huang resigned last week from Nvidia, apparently ousted by the board after revelations that he had extramarital affairs, both with a Chinese businesswoman suspected of being an intelligence asset for Beijing and with a senior executive who reported to him.

The businesswoman Huang was seeing who reportedly had links to Chinese intelligence was an executive at Singapore-based cloud computing firm Megaspeed International, a company being investigated by the U.S. for allegedly violating U.S. export controls by illegally diverting advanced Nvidia AI chips to Chinese companies.

Huang faces questioning by Congress

Also Tuesday, key House lawmakers said that Huang has voluntarily agreed to appear for transcribed interviews as the company faces intensifying national scrutiny over sales of its advanced computer chips.

Huang will appear before congressional committees on Dec. 1, followed by the company’s general counsel, Tim Teter, on Dec. 2.

“We look forward to hearing from Jensen Huang and Tim Teter on their roles in a potential breach of our national security,” said a statement from the chairmen of three House committees with oversight of China and intelligence issues.

“The American people need answers on Mr. Huang and Nvidia’s role in allowing dangerous PRC actors to access our most advanced chips, as well as the role of a potential Chinese intelligence asset in this scandal,” the lawmakers said, referring to the People’s Republic of China.

Meanwhile, Nvidia said it’s creating a new board committee to oversee national security and global trade compliance following the revelations about Huang.

Nvidia’s advanced chips are in high demand because they’re essential for cloud computing and data centers that underpin the AI boom, but they face restrictions on their sale to China. Trump said it was “good” that Nvidia had created the new oversight committee.

“Yes, that’s a good idea. It’s a great company,” Trump said shortly after arriving Tuesday evening in the Philippine capital of Manila, where he participated in a photo opportunity with the leaders of Australia, India and Japan. “It’s a great idea.”

—Kelvin Chan, AP business writer

Associated Press writers Darlene Superville and Kevin Freking in Washington and Josh Boak in Shenzhen, China, contributed.

Anthropic lets apps bill Claude costs to their customers

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Linked billing lets developers choose who pays for each request, but not every customer can use it.

Anthropic has launched Linked billing in beta, letting apps built on Claude bill API usage to a customer’s own Claude organisation. Customers can set a spend cap or unlink the app, but only organisations that pay Anthropic directly qualify, and their own data protections do not carry over.

Apps built on Claude can now charge their AI usage to their customers’ own Claude accounts. The feature, called Linked billing, launched in beta on Tuesday, according to Anthropic’s release notes.

A customer first links its Claude organisation to the app and approves it. The developer then chooses, on each request, whether the customer or the developer pays. The request still runs on the developer’s account, with its usage limits and model access.

Customers can set a monthly spending cap for each app in the Claude Console, or unlink it at any time. If Anthropic cannot bill the customer, the request is refused rather than charged to the developer. Anthropic is strict on limits elsewhere too: it ends enterprise discounts once customers use up their tokens.

Not every customer qualifies. It only works for organisations that pay Anthropic directly, not those billed through AWS, Google Cloud or Azure, or with only a Claude subscription. Credits from Claude plans and Anthropic’s startup programmes cannot be used either.

There is also a privacy catch. A customer’s own data protections, such as zero data retention and HIPAA agreements, do not apply to these requests.

Google expands access to Gemini Tasks, which can help with calls, purchases

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google is expanding the rollout of Tasks, a toggle in Gemini that converts the chatbot into a more active helper.

Unlike Gemini’s Chat, which merely allows users to talk to Google’s AI assistant for informational purposes, Tasks empowers the app to complete basic secretarial functions, deputizing it as a kind of personal assistant.

How, specifically, can you use it? Google says that the app can call businesses and book appointments on users’ behalf; it can also complete purchases for them via Google Pay, and even sign into websites securely if prompted to do so.

In a blog post, Josh Woodword, VP of Gemini, claims to have recently used the feature to call local car dealerships to schedule a service appointment for his family’s minivan. He says he also used it to analyze his electrical bill.

Getting the function up and running is easy. Users merely flip a toggle within the app from Chat to Tasks, which enables the feature. Users can also send texts to Gemini or call the app to give it commands.

Seattle tech vet and Sounders FC minority owner Chee Chew joins front office to lead AI push

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

by Kurt Schlosser on Nov 17, 2026 at 11:02 amNovember 17, 2026 at 11:02 am

Chee Chew helped build the Windows 95 Start menu, started Google Hangouts and ran consumer engagement at Amazon. Now the Seattle tech veteran is taking on a new job with the Sounders and Reign FC, where he’ll lead how the soccer clubs use technology, data and AI.

Chew, a minority owner of Sounders FC since 2019, is joining the organization’s executive team as chief technology and transformation officer, reporting to majority owner Adrian Hanauer, the club announced Tuesday.

“His knowledge of and passion for the Sounders and Reign allow him to hit the ground running,” Hanauer said in a statement. “His experience leading teams through major shifts in technology gives us a unique opportunity to strengthen both the soccer and business sides of our organization.”

Chew most recently was chief product and technology officer at Zapier, where he helped steer the workflow automation company toward AI. Before that he was chief product officer at mParticle and at Twilio, where he oversaw the communications company’s $3.2 billion acquisition of Segment.

He spent four years at Amazon, nearly eight years as a vice president of engineering at Google, where he also led the company’s Seattle and Kirkland offices, and 14 years at Microsoft working on Internet Explorer, Windows Mobile and Xbox Live.

Chew said his direct team will stay small, but its work will reach every corner of the business, including HR, ticket sales, customer service, match-day planning, corporate partnerships, legal, and player recruiting and scouting. He also wants every department to have its own “builders,” staffers who can create AI tools and automations for their own teams.

“In order to do a change like this, it’s more than technology,” Chew told GeekWire. “Technology is in many ways the easiest part. Driving change, getting deep, building side by side with folks, I think is what it takes.”

Chew didn’t grow up playing soccer. “My family was a very traditional Chinese family of study and play piano,” he said. “It was a stereotype, but it was true for my family.” He caught the bug in college, where he played a ton of intramurals, and later from his wife, Christine, who played through college, and their two kids.

The couple joined the Sounders ownership group in 2019 as one of 11 Seattle-rooted families, many with deep ties to the region’s tech industry. Former Microsoft executive Terry Myerson pulled the group together, and it included Microsoft CEO Satya Nadella, CFO Amy Hood, and others.

A group of owners has met every other Friday since 2019, and “leveraging technology has always been part of the strategy” for the club, Chew said.

The job grew out of a demo Chew built for Hanauer about two and a half years ago: an AI agent that could handle complicated fan requests, such as a season ticket holder with four seats who wants to buy a block of eight. The club later brought in consultants to train staff on AI, but the effort never quite gained momentum, he said.

Talks about a bigger role started early this year and came together by midyear. “Adrian and I talked about it, and we agreed for me to come on board and take an operational role and hack on some code,” Chew said.

“I think I’ve created more code in the past six months than I did in the past 20 years. It’s pretty amazing. I’m having a ton of fun.”

Chew describes Hanauer as a visionary on “the art of the possible” who comes in daily asking, “Could you do this?” Recently, as Chew was boarding a flight home to Seattle, Hanauer called from a meeting where staff doubted they could scale up one effort tenfold without hiring more people. Could AI help?

On the flight, while using the plane’s Wi-Fi, he built a prototype with Anthropic’s Claude. “By the time I landed, I sent it to them and said, ‘OK, give this a try,’” he said. The club used it in an experiment to scale up performance marketing before a match.

It’s the kind of hands-on building Chew says he’s been doing a lot of lately.

“I think I’ve created more code in the past six months than I did in the past 20 years,” he said. “It’s pretty amazing. I’m having a ton of fun.”

He recently ran an AI analysis of one player’s game, based on stats such as touches, expected goals and distance run. It pointed out strengths and areas to improve. The player was “very hungry for it,” Chew said, and asked if they could do it every game.

The club’s performance analytics group, led by data analyst Tyler Cox, was already ahead of much of the organization, and Chew said he’s helping speed up that work with AI.

The bigger challenge is getting people to change how they work, Chew said. At Zapier, he saw many companies struggle to get results from AI, with some spending “massive token budgets” without a big win. He said the answer is blending AI with traditional automation and fitting it into how people already work.

“It has to be approachable for folks,” he said. “You can’t go in and just throw everything up and start from scratch.”

The Sounders host Real Salt Lake in the wild-card round of the MLS Cup Playoffs on Thursday at Lumen Field. Asked whether AI could help figure out a win, Chew passed.

“I don’t want to get involved and interfere with our coaching staff,” he said.

Apple confirms iCloud sign-in issues, App Store and Apple Pay outages

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Some Apple users are having trouble signing into their accounts, downloading apps, and accessing music on Tuesday, Nov. 17, just days after a banking disruption and a United Airlines system outage caused problems for customers and travelers.

The Apple disruptions add to a busy stretch of technical failures. Last week, a Microsoft outage disrupted Outlook and other Microsoft 365 services, while a separate telecommunications failure prompted a rare nationwide FAA ground stop. (There's no established connection between those incidents and Tuesday’s Apple problems. But there sure are conspiracy theories.) FEMA will also conduct a scheduled test of the emergency alert system on Tuesday afternoon, adding to the sense of generalized anxiety.

Apple's System Status page lists active outages affecting the App Store, Apple Music, and AppleCare on Device, which lets users purchase AppleCare coverage through their devices. It also flags an “issue” affecting iCloud Account & Sign In. All four disruptions remained ongoing when checked at 1:50 p.m. ET, while Apple Pay was listed as available.

For the App Store, Apple Music, and iCloud sign-in, Apple warns that the services "may be slow or unavailable." The company lists those incidents as beginning at 11:45 a.m. ET. The AppleCare purchase outage began earlier, at 11:15 a.m. ET. Meanwhile, Downdetector shows an uptick in user reports involving Apple Pay, Apple Support, and iCloud. Apple has not provided a cause or an estimate for restoring the affected services. Users can check the company's System Status page for updates.

Bitcoin's $90,000 Comeback Now Unlikely, Novogratz Warns

📅 2026-11-17  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Galaxy Digital CEO Mike Novogratz said that the odds of Bitcoin hitting $90,000 by the end of 2026 are slim in light of prevailing macroeconomic headwinds and the growing likelihood that the Federal Reserve may hike interest rates.

During a live event with Anthony Scaramucci at the Digital Assets Forum in New York City, Novogratz was asked if Bitcoin could get back to $90,000 by the end of this year.

"Less likely than it was. You need something to happen," Novogratz said.

His gloomy outlook comes as Bitcoin has struggled to rebound after a devastating drop that saw its price dip as low as $62,000 over the weekend. Bitcoin's price stood at around $64,600 on Nov. 17. The largest cryptocurrency by market capitalization has declined for six consecutive weeks.

A shift in the U.S. monetary policy outlook, namely the prospect of more rate hikes from the Federal Reserve, has been largely to blame for Bitcoin's decline, according to Novogratz.

"At the start of the year, everybody thought the Fed was going to be cutting now," he said.

But high inflation and ongoing supply shocks have forced investors to readjust their expectations.

"Inflation's going up. We've got inflation going up," Novogratz said, warning that inflationary pressures could become increasingly entrenched.

"The Fed's going to hike. At least two more, probably three more," he said.

Novogratz predicted that the Fed would raise rates by 25 basis points in December and then hike again at its next meeting.

He said that higher rates, at least in the near term, tend to weigh on Bitcoin. But the higher cost of government borrowing could eventually provide support for the cryptocurrency.

"Yeah, later," he said in response to the suggestion that higher borrowing costs could be a tailwind for Bitcoin.

"First, you're gonna hike rates. Oh, sell gold, right? You're going to go to the dollar. It's so ingrained in peoples' heads," he said.

"Carry costs going up," he said. Bitcoin doesn't offer the same yield as interest-bearing dollar assets.

"You don't make any interest on Bitcoin in dollars," he said.

In particular, Novogratz said that the massive rise in Treasury yields has been particularly detrimental to the crypto market.

"Literally 40 basis points in like four days of trading. So that's why crypto's down," he said.

He also said that the massive crypto hack could still be weighing on investors' minds, adding to market jitters.

"I think in the back of their minds is the hack," he said.
